# Chapter 3Blockchain TechnologyAiding Transformation in the Healthcare and Medical Industry

Navneet Kaur and Naresh Kshetri

DOI: [10.1201/9781003449515-3](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781003449515-3)

## 3.1 Introduction

The term “blockchain” is one of the most popular buzzwords in the world of medical technology. It has the power to revolutionize the medical industry, as when fully implemented, it can completely revamp operations, putting patients at the center of everything while simultaneously improving security, privacy, and accessibility.

A decentralized and widely distributed database architecture known as the distributed ledger enables several autonomous and self-governing domains to collaborate, coordinate, and take part in a logical decision-making process. The system achieves the properties through a sequence of time-stamped blocks that cannot be changed arbitrarily. Instead, the blocks can only be changed or added through a consensus protocol that entails solving a mathematical equation (called mining) until it meets a pre-established criterion. Blockchain's potential in the medical and healthcare sector is still in their infancy, with numerous research and advancements being made in related technologies. A healthcare database's core components include security, interoperability, data sharing, and data access. As security is at the core of blockchain, its properties—such as immutability, ensured authentication, centralized access, and storage—make it a promising solution.

The medical industry has struggled to securely communicate and access patient data due to its fragmented and dispersed information. Blockchain technology can help address this issue by enabling highly configurable openness while maintaining high-security levels. Leading businesses are paving the way for a blockchain revolution, which can improve the quality of life by enhancing care and treatment. The centralized environment of the medical industry has led to misdiagnosis and mistreatment due to the fragmented and dispersed health information.

The overall healthcare ecosystem's interoperability is a problem, and the vulnerabilities to cyberthreats and assaults make it even more difficult to safeguard patient data. Recent studies show how vulnerable the healthcare sector is to cyberattacks \[[1](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_1), [2](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_2)\]. Information can be lost by ransomware or denial of service attacks, and in many circumstances, the information cannot be recovered. Additionally, since accessing patient medical records jeopardizes reputation, insurance, and money, among other aspects of the healthcare ecosystem, security is a necessary component. Breaches can destroy health systems, undermine patient trust, and endanger human life \[[3](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_3)\]. In healthcare systems, the information is both proprietary and confidential. Therefore, the blockchain technology's implementation in the medical industry would not only automate the integrity of data exchange but also give users the ability to verify whether their data has been altered and how.

Concisely, blockchain can improve digital health by making it simpler to transmit data securely, with patient agreement, across incredibly dispersed healthcare systems. Due to its openness and prominent level of security, numerous uses of blockchain technology are possible in the medical and health sectors. Since it is challenging to determine the state of the art due to the constantly evolving of new Blockchain (BC) solutions and the growth of healthcare systems, we propose carrying out a comprehensive literature review. The current systemic study aims to help academics understand the alternatives and limitations in healthcare blockchain applications by offering insightful information about the technology landscape. Here are some highlights of this chapter's significant contributions. First, we'll go over the history of blockchain technology as well as its salient characteristics and advantages. We'll also talk about some internal applications and how blockchain is being used in the healthcare sector. Last, we draw attention to the difficulties in analyzing both practical and intangible problems in blockchain applications. The remaining part of this book chapter is structured as follows: A synopsis of our goals for this chapter may be found in [Section 3.1](#chapter3.xhtml#sec3_1). In [Section 3.2](#chapter3.xhtml#sec3_2), we provided a high-level review of blockchain characteristics and operational concepts. [Section 3.3](#chapter3.xhtml#sec3_3) addressed the need of blockchain in the healthcare sector. [Section 3.4](#chapter3.xhtml#sec3_4) gives a high-level overview on the importance of implementing blockchain. [Section 3.5](#chapter3.xhtml#sec3_5) presents information on the existing uses case and applications. [Section 3.6](#chapter3.xhtml#sec3_6) highlights the functional issues in blockchain applications. [Section 3.7](#chapter3.xhtml#sec3_7) is devoted to the conclusion. Finally, [Section 3.8](#chapter3.xhtml#sec3_8) provides future research directions.

## 3.2 Background

### 3.2.1 Blockchain Overview

One of the most significant inventions of the twenty-first century is blockchain technology which has captivated the attention and money of the financial services organizations. It improves trackability and visibility throughout the supply chains of various industries, in addition to offering operational and regulatory verification efficiencies. Blockchain is a system of distributed ledgers that enables the safe and reliable preservation of any kind of data format by allowing peer-to-peer networked encrypted transactions to be transmitted to all users either publicly or privately \[[4](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_4)\]. An increasing quantity of data, referred to as blocks, is connected to one another securely using encryption to form a blockchain. Every block contains a timestamp, a secured encryption of the previous block, and transactional data. The data related to transactions was present at the moment the block was created, according to the timestamp. Because every block builds upon the information found in the previous one, they are interconnected and form a structure akin to a chain. The blockchain works by sequentially adding secure transaction blocks to a chain and simultaneously transmitting the updated chain to network nodes. As a result, since the updated blockchain is consistently disseminated, the transactions included within each individual block are unchangeable, and the blockchain's integrity can be checked. Individuals involved in a transaction use public identifying keys, and the payer cryptographically signs transactions using a secret private key that is known only to the payer. This procedure creates a mathematical puzzle out of unfinished transactions. Using computers, “miners” (people) generate a hash—a distinct string of characters and numbers for every block—to solve the challenge \[[5](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_5)\].

Blockchain allows for auditing and tracing to link a new block to its predecessor. A distributed network of peers connects nodes, or entities without an established trust relationship, to form the management of the blockchain system. To be useful, the blockchain must have some way for nodes to mutually agree on the next legitimate in the chain of blocks. The two most often used strategies for achieving such a distributed agreement are as follows as shown in [Table 3.1](#chapter3.xhtml#tbl3_1):

 [Table 3.1](#chapter3.xhtml#b3tbl3_1) Advantages and disadvantages of two used strategies for achieving a distributed agreement 

 
| Strategies || Advantages || Disadvantages |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Proof of work || Power and control in the network are completely decentralized || Expensive processing power and high energy consumption |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Increased network security || Small networks are vulnerable to attacks |
| Proof-of-stake || More efficient in terms of energy || A less decentralized network than PoW |
| Greater incentives for higher stakes || Less secure than PoW |
| Transactions are processed more quickly ||  |

*   Proof-of-work is the classic block validation mechanism in which nodes compete to generate the subsequent block by investing computational power to solve a difficult mathematical problem. The first node to reach a solution broadcast it to the network; the solution is validated by the rest of the nodes, and work on the next block begins.
    
*   Proof-of-stake techniques avoid the computational problem by allowing just a randomly selected subset of nodes to produce each block. The likelihood of selection is weighted by each entity's amount of existing investment in the system, which is often expressed as the value or time span of asset holdings relevant to that specific blockchain.
    

### 3.2.2 Key Features/Characteristics

*   Decentralization is a key component of blockchain technology. Every piece of content uploaded to the blockchain follows a network of peers and employs one of the several consensus minimizing strategies; the content is not centralized and is subject to constant oversight.
    
*   Data security is the primary goal of every blockchain transaction. No outside entities are involved in the data transmission process via blockchain, thus there is no chance of unauthorized access or manipulation.
    
*   Another important aspect of blockchain technology is openness. The ledger is made available among multiple individuals. This increases transparency among blockchain network node participants.
    
*   The blockchain is distributable. This enables expanding the number of nodes in the blockchain system to make it less susceptible to being attacked by bad actors. The potential of a bad actor to influence the blockchain's consensus procedure is lessened by increasing the overall number of nodes.
    
*   Another important aspect of the blockchain is persistence. Once an entry has been recognized in the blockchain, it cannot be erased because the shared ledger is stored across numerous nodes. Also, blockchains are cryptographically safe, making sure the data held throughout the ledger is not altered with and is attestable.
    
*   The fact that the pseudonymity blockchain is utilized by numerous blockchains is another intriguing aspect of it.
    

### 3.2.3 Different Kinds of Blockchain

The different kinds of blockchain are shown in [Figure 3.1](#chapter3.xhtml#fig3_1).

![Figure 3.1 Various blockchain types: Consortium, private, public, and hybrid.](images/fig3_1.jpg)

[Figure 3.1](#chapter3.xhtml#b3fig3_1) Various blockchain types: Consortium, private, public, and hybrid.

### 3.2.4 Benefits of Blockchain

The benefits of blockchain are described below and also shown in [Figure 3.2](#chapter3.xhtml#fig3_2).

![Figure 3.2 Benefits of blockchain technology.](images/fig3_2.jpg)

[Figure 3.2](#chapter3.xhtml#b3fig3_2) Benefits of blockchain technology.

#### 3.2.4.1 Decentralization

The decentralized storage system is a crucial part of this technology, enhancing security and data authentication. In order to create a network or chain, each block carries transaction data, the preceding block's cryptographic hash, and a timestamp. Any changes in data could trigger a chain reaction, leading to a freeze. Once processed, each machine locks simultaneously, creating an immutable record of the data. Decentralized applications can be created using the blockchain framework or existing platforms like Hyperledger and Ethereum. These platforms enable programmers to build additional blockchain apps on over already existing ones and generate new test nets in accordance with the protocol.

#### 3.2.4.2 Trust and Transparency

After the new block has been properly incorporated into the blockchain, the revised version will be made available to all participants. Invalid data blocks are not added to the blockchain; they are instead discarded. Participants use consensus techniques as validation tools to confirm the legitimacy and validity of a data block. Prospective users may view and amend data that has been captured and stored on the blockchain instantaneously. Data theft or tampering can be prevented by blockchain building trust and transparency among the clients.

#### 3.2.4.3 Privacy and Security

The blockchain protects transactional data and block data from malicious, internal, hostile, and accidental threats. Services, policy, and technology in the fields of security along with information technology are frequently employed to identify potential threats, stop them before they happen, and react appropriately when they do.

Blockchain privacy is the capacity of a stakeholder or group to isolate themselves from other's data and to be able to interact in a comprehensible way. The transactions can be carried out without revealing personal information in the blockchain network creating a private environment. Users can also maintain compliance by carefully self-divulging \[[6](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_6), [7](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_7)\].

#### 3.2.4.4 Accessibility and Durability

Blockchain technology offers total security. It maintains identical blocks of medical data across the network and lacks a single point of failure because it cannot be owned by a centralized authority. Because there is no centralized authority, all participating nodes’ resources are used, preventing many-to-one traffic flows and ensuring flexibility and resilience. This approach also eliminates waiting times and addresses the problem of a single failed step \[[8](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_8)\].

## 3.3 Data Transparency and Security Needs in Medical Sector

Healthcare data demands a high level of security and privacy. Privacy is the beginning point for defining who and what should have access to confidential patient's information \[[4](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_4)\]. In response to this issue, several security standards, such as Health Insurance Portability and Accountability Act (HIPAA), Control Objectives for Information and Related Technology (COBIT), and District Integrated Strategy for HIV / Aids (DISHA), have been established and implemented to protect patients health information. This also necessitates agreement between healthcare providers and authorities for the adoption of accepted norms and procedures.

Healthcare providers have been increasingly reliant on smart technologies since the advent of the Healthcare 4.0 era. Such technologies aid in the treatment and diagnosis of patient ailments and the transmission, reception, and collection of patient data for health record management. The quality of data recorded on electronic health record (EHR) systems is crucial to a healthcare provider; such data must be always free of risks and safe. With the advent of big data, the number and complexity of healthcare records have increased and are still not optimized. Records are frequently duplicated, mismatched using different identities/naming conventions, and made available on many networks and directories of healthcare systems. The security of healthcare records is becoming increasingly crucial to protect data from security breaches and criminal activity. Unauthorized users can trade or expose patient data to the public, giving anybody with access to the data-sensitive knowledge about the patients. Addresses, phone numbers, full names, and other personal information may be included.

The confidentiality of patients’ data is critical to good healthcare management \[[7](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_7)\]. In response to these problems, some nations have suggested or established regulatory norms for healthcare systems to minimize cyber risks, thereby improving patient confidentiality and confidence in the provider-patient relationship. Most healthcare systems nowadays have centralized client-server designs, with a central authority having complete access to the system. In this case, a lack of privacy or security problems may cause system breakdowns, potentially allowing cyber criminals access to patient data.

The healthcare system is quite complicated and has many different domains, each of which includes doctors, researchers, practitioners, support personnel, management staff, and the patients \[[9](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_9)\]. This makes organizing and maintaining patient data a difficult undertaking that comes with high maintenance and upkeep expenses \[[10](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_10)\]. Different data architectures and distinct operations in various healthcare sectors further exacerbate this problem. These factors make it highly challenging and difficult to effectively exchange information about healthcare across different healthcare sectors. Thereby, there is a need for a system which can manage, maintain, and administer healthcare information and records. Both traditional personal and EHR systems maintained by a third-party vendor still pose a significant trust, privacy, and data security issues. Due to privacy and data security concerns, the conventional electronic healthcare approach lacks transparency and cannot meet the privacy needs of stakeholders. Blockchain technology presents substantial possibilities for addressing the grave issue of huge and very diversified data in healthcare systems as well as security-related problems \[[11](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_11)\].

## 3.4 Significance of Integrating Blockchain Technology into the Healthcare System

Blockchain technology is transforming healthcare application governance and data management paradigms. This is mostly because of its versatility and special capacity to split, distribute, and safeguard medical information. Data security is a major problem for the healthcare sector. Due to outdated legacy IT design, the majority of healthcare organizations keep private medical records in one central location that is susceptible to espionage and other security breaches. As a matter of fact, blockchain-based technology may be useful for organizing, processing, and distributing personal medical records; it might also help with data security concerns and support the digitalization of the healthcare sector \[[12](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_12)\].

Additionally, the healthcare industry is currently head starting to develop infrastructure, computer programmers, and strategic strategies that can reliably and consistently combine the various kinds of data that are available to them \[[13](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_13)\]. Blockchain is flexible in its application, and this flexibility stems from its capacity to be applied to a variety of operations. Blockchain-based solutions has improved communication and transparency and brought significant advantages to patients and healthcare providers \[[14](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_14)\]. The primary focus of the healthcare industry is on using contemporary technology to enhance the efficacy and efficiency of healthcare systems.

There are stages to incorporating blockchain into the healthcare industry. In the initial phase, medical professionals have unprecedented access to the blockchain; all clinical data is monitored and preserved inside the current health information technology systems. Through Application Programming Interfaces (APIs), patient IDs are utilized to supply the blockchain network with a variety of patient-related data. The blockchain system's smart contract is then used to complete the incoming transactions. All blockchain network transactions are carried out with patient public IDs, which are devoid of any personal data. The blocks are constructed and linked together using the immutable ledger. After that, all transactions are committed and uniquely recognized. The health provider uses APIs to initiate query processing or reverse mining as a result. The block database only contains non-identifiable patient data, such as age, gender, and conditions. Clinical data is analyzed to gain fresh insights. Finally, if the patient decides to reveal his or her identity with the healthcare professional, the private key can be shared. This allows the clinician to access the patient's data and provide solutions or therapy for symptoms that have been detected. Naturally, those without the patient's confidential password are unable to access the information \[[15](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_15)\]. In this part, we will look at some common applications and advantages of distributed ledger technology in the field of healthcare.

### 3.4.1 Enhancing Telehealth's Credibility

The safety and confidentiality of data are becoming more and more of a worry as telehealth services gain popularity. Between patients, medical equipment, and healthcare providers, blockchain technology can establish a digital trust chain to ensure safe transmission of EHR, with authorized network participants. Self-executing smart contracts, which operate automatically and only act when a predefined condition is satisfied, are another way that technology based on blockchain may improve reliability and confidence in the healthcare sector. Blockchain transactions are encrypted, preventing third-party manipulation, fostering trust and transparency due to their immutability and real-time visibility, ensuring no one can manipulate data for their own benefit.

### 3.4.2 Verification of Identity

Blockchain technology offers a secure method for identity verification, as it uses encrypted ledgers with unique authorization keys. The Estonian Government and Dutch data security company “Guardtime” have developed a blockchain system for patient ID verification, where each smartcard is connected to a block in the network, serving as the authentication key for data access or update.

### 3.4.3 Clinical Studies

Blockchain can transform clinical trial accountability and transparency by improving data integrity and promoting trust among stakeholders. It ensures unchangeable data records, preventing drug companies from altering trial results. Blockchain architecture can be customized to add only confirmed data points, enabling peer review without data owners sharing or relinquishing control. This approach addresses issues of verifiable data integrity, data interchange, and cooperation confidence in clinical trials.

### 3.4.4 Traceability of Drugs and Visibility of the Supply Chain

One in ten medical supplies in nations with moderate to low incomes are either inferior or fake, making counterfeit pharmaceuticals a serious threat to public health and safety. The ongoing epidemic has exacerbated these issues. Blockchain technology can improve supply chain visibility by tracking the origin and validity of pharmaceutical products across distributed networks. Its immutable data and shareable ledger can help reduce fraud and establish a system for product verification. This could potentially help identify and report fake or illegal pharmaceuticals. International Business Machine (IBM) Blockchain Transparent Supply solution provides product-specific transparency across supply chain partners, ensuring supply chain integrity, combating theft and fraud, reducing costs and wait times, and addressing legal specifications for drug tracking, all while utilizing data and digitization.

### 3.4.5 Claims for Insurance

Challenges in the insurance market include building trust, managing data, and tackling identity theft. Blockchain technology can help combat fraudulent claims, automate claim processing, and reduce identity theft in the healthcare sector. As an example:

*   Blockchain networks prevent transaction immutability, ensuring only authorized peers can access data, reducing fraud risk.
    
*   Smart contracts automate claim processing, improving customer experience.
    
*   Consumers still have access to their identity information due to no central server, and all value chain participants can see changes, reducing identity theft risks.
    

### 3.4.6 Feasibility to Share Information

Blockchain networks can be replicated to other sites within the same facility or medical service network, enabling accurate, reliable, and consistent healthcare data sharing. This technology allows researchers, partner facilities, and insurance companies to upload data to a blockchain in one area and disseminate it to multiple sites within the same network. The new locations also share the data, distributing it across the network and allowing location access to the latest information.

## 3.5 Blockchain Technology Applications in Healthcare

Originally created for use in cryptocurrency and economics, blockchain technology is currently being used in several fields, including genetics, medicine, telemedicine, e-health, telemonitoring, personalized healthcare, and neuroscience. It is a prominent participant in several industries thanks to its capacity to protect and stabilize data sets, which enables a variety of transaction kinds. The following points mentioned below discuss some medical and healthcare fields where blockchain technology has considerable potential.

### 3.5.1 Blockchain Technology for Electronic Health Records

The digitization of medical documentations has become crucial due to the increasing need for efficient decision-making in healthcare. Blockchain technology is being used to preserve EHRs, which were not designed to manage lifetime records across multiple institutions. This solution allows patients to access their current and historical healthcare data, reducing the dispersion of data across different institutions. Academics argue that blockchain technology can provide an innovative way to handle EHRs.

“MedRec” is a platform powered by the blockchain that facilitates data interchange, authentication, secrecy, and integrity. It provides users with convenient access to personal health records and medical records from many doctors and treatment centers \[[16](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_16)\]. “MedRec” stores medical records without any storage or adjustments. It provides patients with advice and stores a record mark, allowing the patient to control its location. This system transfers control from the institution to the patient, strengthening their role as owner. Administration associations act as patient agents for patients who prefer not to handle their information. A user interface is also integrated into the MedRec architecture to improve the long-term linkage with medical records from other organizations.

MeDShare is a secure blockchain framework for healthcare information sharing that was developed by Xia et al. \[[17](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_17)\]. It addresses issues with EHR adoption such as protected data tracking, provenance, auditing, and loss of management. MeDShare provides stronger data provenance, individualized audit control, and fewer security risks, making it an ideal solution for EHR sharing among cloud service providers and hospitals.

EHR data is crucial for accurate diagnoses and effective treatment, but its storage, transmission, and distribution can pose serious risks to patients’ health. Chronic conditions like cancer and HIV can increase the prevalence of such hazards. Thus, maintaining up-to-date patient records is crucial to guaranteeing that patients receive the right care, particularly for those who require extensive follow-up, rehabilitation, and pre- and post-treatment procedures.

Blockchain technology can be applied in clinical settings, as demonstrated by Dubovitskaya et al.'s \[[18](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_18)\] proposal of a permissioned blockchain system for encrypted data storage as well as access control, which allows for the management, upkeep, and sharing of electronic medical information pertaining to cancer patients.

Rapid advancements in blockchain technology have placed Estonia at the forefront of the space thanks to its medical record effort, which used blockchain technology to keep several million medical information confidential while allowing access to physicians and insurance firms \[[19](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_19)\].

Blockchain technology in medicine is driven by the promise of unchangeable healthcare records for patients. The technology allows for swift identification and labeling of any attempts at access or modification, ensuring patient integrity and detecting illegal activities like fraud or record-adulteration. It also simplifies exchanging and reviewing records for licensed pharmaceutical services, allowing suppliers to be aware of patient visits. The use of blockchain technology in patient-caring algorithms can quickly account for drugs bugs, hypersensitivities, and drugs solutions. This enhances clinical data confirmation, security, medical record management, and care planning efficiency without requiring the time-consuming completion of drug compromise forms \[[20](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_20)\].

### 3.5.2 Blockchain Technology for Clinical Research

Clinical studies face challenges like data privacy, integrity, sharing, record keeping, and patient enrollment \[[21](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_21)\]. These problems can be solved by blockchain technology, Artificial Intelligence, or AI, and machine learning. Blockchain is being used by healthcare researchers more and more to solve these problems, which is opening the door for a big change in the healthcare industry \[[22](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_22)\].

Timothy et al.'s \[[23](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_23)\] study suggests that permissioned Ethereum, a blockchain-based protocol with smart contract capabilities \[[24](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_24), [25](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_25)\], can be used alongside clinic-based data management systems to address patient enrollment issues. The study found that Ethereum transactions completed faster than Bitcoin transactions, suggesting that Ethereum smart contracts could increase data transparency in clinical trials, particularly in patient enrollment via blockchains.

Mehdi Benchoufi's \[[26](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_26)\] study utilized blockchain technology to design a framework for obtaining patients’ informed consent for tracking, ensuring a safe, publicly verifiable, and unfalsifiable method for tracking.

### 3.5.3 Blockchain Technology for Detecting Medical Fraud

Blockchain technology is being utilized in the medical sector to manage the supply chain for prescription drugs, a crucial concern due to the complexity of the industry. Any breach in the supply chain can negatively impact a patient's wellbeing \[[27](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_27)\]. Blockchains offer data transparency and product traceability, providing a secure platform to prevent fraud and improve supply chain security. However, manipulation of blockchains is challenging as records can only be validated and modified through smart contracts \[[28](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_28)\]. Overall, blockchain technology is a valuable tool in the healthcare sector.

### 3.5.4 Blockchain Technology in Neuroscience

With the goal of removing mechanical connections with the outside world and enabling control of items and data by mental instructions, neurology is an important topic in blockchain applications \[[29](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_29)\]. These neurological devices may utilize neural activity to infer the person's mental state, in addition to analyzing patterns of cerebral activity and converting them into orders for external equipment. They use neural interface devices with sensors, computation processors, and wireless connectivity to read and interpret brain impulses.

Neurogress, a company based in Geneva, is utilizing blockchain technology to interpret the brain's electrical activity and communicate it to controlled equipment. The company uses a single device on the head to store brain impulses on a neural interface. Neurogress, a company that specializes in creating neural-control systems for devices such as Augmented Reality / Virtual Reality (AR/VR), robotic arms, smart appliances, and drones maintains 90% accuracy in brain reading using a machine learning model. This requires copious amounts of user cerebral activity data for training purposes, leading to significant storage needs. Neurogress plans to adopt blockchain to address data storage security and privacy issues.

Blockchain technology offers a decentralized approach to user data, making it more secure and transparent. This guarantees the protection and privacy of personal data by making the Neurogress platform visible and accessible to potential users. Blockchains have significant applications in the future, such as brain augmentation, simulation, and thinking. The need for a storage medium arises from digitizing a full human brain, making blockchain technology relevant. One recommendation is to save mind files in a collaborative file system. This way, the files can enable historical versioning and be utilized as building blocks for distinct idea chains.

### 3.5.5 Blockchain Technology for Research and the Pharmaceutical Industry

The chemical and pharmaceutical industry is a rapidly growing sector in healthcare, introducing new drugs and ensuring the safety of medical supplies. It supports safe medication examination and processing, promoting faster patient recovery. However, timely product tracking can pose risks due to counterfeiters and phony pharmaceuticals, highlighting the need for improved supply chain management.

Fake medication production and distribution pose significant global health risks, especially in impoverished nations. Hyperledger \[[30](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_30)\], a research foundation, has developed a blockchain-based system to assess, oversee, and ensure pharmaceutical production processes during development, production, and R&D, aiming to combat and verify the creation of fake medications. Digital technology is vital for the production and shipment of pharmaceutical items, particularly in developing nations, in order to ensure the effective distribution of legitimate and dependable drugs. Implementing a digital drug control system, or DDCS \[[31](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_31)\], might help curb the sale of fake drugs. Sanofi, Pfizer, and Amgen, three of the largest pharmaceutical corporations, have teamed together to launch an integrated pilot project that leverages blockchain-based DDCS for the inspection and assessment of innovative medications \[[32](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_32)\]. This would boost the credibility of the drug supply chain \[[33](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_33)\], improve the monitoring of counterfeit medications \[[34](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_34)\], and guarantee the effectiveness of pharmaceuticals given to customers or end users.

## 3.6 Blockchain Technology: Challenges and Limitations

Blockchain is a technology that is gaining traction in several industries and offers many advantages and prospects. However, this technology has a unique set of issues and limitations, just like any other modern technology. Some of these are listed below and shown in [Figure 3.3](#chapter3.xhtml#fig3_3).

![Figure 3.3 Key challenges and opportunities of blockchain adoption.](images/fig3_3.jpg)

[Figure 3.3](#chapter3.xhtml#b3fig3_3) Key challenges and opportunities of blockchain adoption.

### 3.6.1 Data Privacy and Security

Although blockchain technology eliminates the need for middlemen in transactions, it also exposes data to potential confidentiality and security threats. Because the blockchain system allows everyone in the organization to verify records, data privacy is compromised because all nodes may view the data provided by one node \[[35](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_35)\]. In emergency situations, patients must choose representatives to access their information and medical history, posing a risk to data privacy and security. High-security techniques prevent data transfer between blocks, and blockchain networks are vulnerable to a 51% attack, making it difficult to transfer data between blocks. This can lead to partial or incomplete access to patient details. A group of miners controlling over 50% of the blockchain network's blocks has been responsible for an attack that has affected five cryptocurrencies. The attack allows miners to control the network without consent, potentially stopping new transactions.

A solution could be to use a lightweight digital signature technique \[[36](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_36)\] to ensure data integrity, transmitting a receipt to the patient. The anonymization of patient medical data is crucial due to its sensitive nature. A lightweight ring structure and digital signatures \[[36](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_36)\] can be used to ensure anonymity, allowing signers to sign data anonymously. Double encryption systems or key exchange methods, like the Diffie-Hellman key exchange technique, can also prevent tampering with medical devices or health data.

### 3.6.2 Taking Care of Storage Capacity

Blockchain, initially designed for transaction data storage, has faced challenges in managing storage capacity due to its expansion into the healthcare industry. With a significant volume of data, including patient records, health histories, and medical images, requiring storage space, blockchain applications require significant storage \[[37](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_37), [38](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_38)\]. Additionally, the rapid expansion of transaction-based databases makes them unsuitable for transactions requiring speed. Therefore, a blockchain solution must be both scalable and robust to handle the increasing volume of data in the healthcare sector. The author \[[11](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_11)\] suggests using cloud storage systems with cryptographic security, digital signatures, and encryption schemes to address the issue of transaction security. They propose storing transactions in separate blocks and using Merkle tree to create a combined hash, thereby maintaining trust among third parties.

### 3.6.3 Issues with Interoperability

Blockchains face challenges in interoperability, which hinders data sharing in the healthcare ecosystem. With multiple stakeholders with varying security and privacy requirements, sharing medical data generated by distant devices is challenging. Medical facilities cannot afford to modernize or digitize to deliver remote services, leading to lack of visibility and inability to trace transactions or data saved on servers. This issue affects specialists and patient careers, making it difficult to effectively share medical information \[[39](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_39)\].

### 3.6.4 Data Sharing

Blockchain technologies are crucial for communicating health information, but security concerns remain. Historically, data records have been kept unavailable, except for those directly involved in patient treatment. But blockchains’ privacy paradigm is more like medical research, keeping patients private or anonymous. To reduce the risk of linking public keys to patients, efforts must be made to reduce the likelihood of analysis linking public keys to patients. Blockchains’ decentralized and transparent structure can prevent data theft, but users may still be traceable through pseudonymous identities or data patterns.

Blockchain architecture's core value is a non-modifiable database of transactions, but it may weaken the anonymity of protected health information (PHI). To address this, de-identified and encrypted information can be added to the blockchain, ensuring secure and HIPAA-compliant PHI. Access to this data is restricted to specific professionals using smart contracts and a secret private key. Maintaining this key discreetly is like keeping a social security number or healthcare records secure. Blockchain technology's strength lies in its ability to confirm transactions without compromising unique information.

### 3.6.5 Standardization Challenges

Blockchain technology implementations are still in its preliminary stages, therefore there will undoubtedly be difficulties with standardization on the road to its practical application in medicine and healthcare. Organizations would be required to provide a variety of well-authenticated and recognized standards in order to assess the volume, type, and structure of the data exchanged in applications powered by blockchain using a certain authorized set of criteria. The data provided will be examined according to these standards, which will also act as safety precautions.

### 3.6.6 Social Difficulties

Blockchain technology, despite its initial stages, faces social and cultural challenges due to its fundamentally different approach. The medical sector is gradually adopting digitization, but it still has a long way to go before fully embracing recent technologies, especially those like blockchain, which have not yet proven effective in clinical settings. It will take time and effort to convince doctors to embrace technology over paperwork.

The techniques and technologies that are being provided have poor rates of adoption in the health industry \[[39](#chapter3.xhtml#ref3_39)\], which makes them untrustworthy and unfit for providing a complete answer to all healthcare problems.

### 3.6.7 Cost

Blockchain technology for distributed data sharing requires a network of nodes, which can be challenging due to the need for significant support from the medical community and the computational power required for maintaining the network. This can lead to higher hardware costs and energy usage. To address these issues, rewards for blockchain participants can increase utilization. Tokens distributed by blockchain-based apps can be exchanged for services like expanded data storage, faster information transport, or access to a data repository. Tokens can also be traded in digital currencies like Bitcoin or US dollars for direct financial remuneration.

Finally, Confidential medical data is currently protected by individuals and institutions, but the shift to decentralized blockchain storage and delivery may face opposition from organizations that respect medical data ownership. Transferring ownership of personal health records to patients will require a paradigm shift in health legislation and policies, necessitating a shift in the way medical records are shared.

### 3.6.8 Technical Difficulties

Finally, aside from technological and regulatory issues, the suggested framework's practical utility would be highly influenced by the end-user experience. Blockchain transactions incorporate cryptographic ideas that we expect most people to be unfamiliar with. Nonetheless, the suggested approach necessitates patients creating and managing key pairs, providing cryptographic signatures, and posting transactions authorizing access to their medical data and records. The intricacies underpinning these operations must be concealed behind a sufficiently user-friendly interface, which will be implemented as a web and/or mobile application. It can be challenging to enhance the end-user experience. The experience of users and healthcare providers must also be considered, and the blockchain does not address broader issues.

## 3.7 Conclusion

Today's world depends heavily on information and communication technology. Unfortunately, there are several problems plaguing the healthcare sector, including a lack of patient-centered initiatives, poor system interoperability, challenges connecting disparate systems, accessibility concerns with EHRs, and issues related to privacy. The use of blockchain in the field of healthcare has drawn more attention in recent years. Because blockchain can manage health information concerns while retaining anonymity, security interoperability, verification, and authentication, it presents a huge potential for the healthcare industry. Healthcare data breaches, unreported clinical trials, and inaccurate data are just a few of the pertinent modern issues that blockchain technology addresses. EHR management may involve several areas of interest for the blockchain.

A wide range of individuals will profit from the practical application of the blockchain technology in the healthcare industry, including medical experts, doctors, R&D specialists, and scientists. The effective sharing of enormous volumes of data, the sharing of knowledge in science, and the sharing of ideas with increased security and guaranteed privacy protection are some of these advantages. Because of the possible data integrity, the healthcare industry could be motivated to adopt blockchain soon to accomplish new goals. With a blockchain-based healthcare framework, people will be more involved in their healthcare, which will improve their quality of life. The usage of the distributed ledger in healthcare institutions is crucial in today's market. It can result in automated procedures for gathering and authenticating data, precise and combined data from many sources that is unchangeable and impervious to tampering, and safe data with a reduced risk of cybercrime. Distributed data is also permitted, along with failure tolerance and redundancy in the system. This study highlights the current problems facing the healthcare industry.

A thorough analysis of benefits and challenges linked to healthcare sector while adopting blockchain has been done in this research. The chapter also focuses on previous studies and their core beliefs and difficulties. We discussed the fundamental principles of blockchain technology and offered an overview of a blockchain implementation that may be used to enable patient-controlled, cross-domain sharing of medical data without the need for centralized authority. There are several distinct advantages to such an approach, and we have especially highlighted how the blockchain meets many of the needs of healthcare systems. Before using a blockchain as a structure for exchanging health information on a broad scale, it will be important to consider the relative benefits of the various options and stay mindful of the technology's very important limitations.

Despite these drawbacks, the results of this systematic review show that there is a sizable but emerging interest in this subject in the body of existing literature, despite the varied ideas and approaches and paucity of practical real-world applications. Smart contracts have demonstrated themselves to be a useful technique for managing transactions between peers on blockchain networks when specific conditions are satisfied. Blockchain technology integration in the healthcare sector can improve outcomes for patients and the whole healthcare system. To get to a more definitive conclusion on the applications and achievements of blockchain in healthcare Smart Contracts (SCs), however, more study with practical applications is required.

## 3.8 Future Scope

The recently developed blockchain idea has been the primary invention driving several important decentralized endeavors, and it is thus reasonable to explore its relevance to the problem of medical industry. Without a question, the successful adoption of blockchain-based systems in clinical healthcare settings will create new opportunities for the progress of biological research. On the contrary hand, the safe, reliable, and scalable gathering, storing, and exchange of this medical information will facilitate the advancement of prospective methods for the identification and management of illnesses in medical precision applications. The adoption of blockchain technology in storing experimental data in neurotechnology is still in an exploratory stage. Only a small number of companies have gone as far availing its benefits of blockchain technology.

The worldwide health IT infrastructure will undergo dramatic changes in the coming years. Emerging technologies will address issues with the current system, such as the lack of interoperability of EHR software, the inadequate and insecure exchange of PHI, an inefficient system of payment for value-based care, and a growing need for patient-centered care. Developing and deploying a blockchain-based healthcare system can address these issues in a revolutionary manner. As modern technologies emerge, therapists must retain an accepting yet inquiring approach, a willingness to put into practice innovative ideas and feedback rather than total dismissal outlook. The medical business stands to gain significantly from blockchain technology. By lowering the expenses of administration, tracking, setup, and having a single data repository, blockchain technology is predicted to significantly improve healthcare in the future, just how the internet changed medical care and gave rise to telemedicine. Since every piece of data will be instantly available as soon as a patient enrolls in a research thanks to the decentralized ledger's availability, using blockchains within medical settings would result in significant processing time savings.

Furthermore, doctors shouldn't be concerned about patients providing them with a false history of health since they can promptly acquire the original, authentic, and high-quality source-documented data. This reduces the possibility of inaccurate medical history information. Patients won't be concerned about seeking another perspective from a different doctor because the data is transparent. Connecting with and learning about many individuals worldwide who have comparable medical issues to one's own would be made feasible by medical records on a distributed ledger. Patients will feel welcomed and encouraged, which will strengthen their resolve to combat the illness in addition to being beneficial to their health. Patients will oversee everything related to their data, especially whom they disclose it with.

# Chapter 4Blockchain Technology Strategy for Cyber Attack and Cyber Crime

Vasudha

DOI: [10.1201/9781003449515-4](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781003449515-4)

## 4.1 Introduction

The blockchain is one of the known technologies that is gaining more and more popularity day by day because of its implementation in various sectors and industrial levels which commonly includes business activities, financial transactions, individual communication, voting, education, and many more \[[1](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_1), [2](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_2)\]. The main highlights of technology are that it provides a proper platform for secure data transfer and also ensures that the transferred data is not tampered which maintains its authenticity and integrity. The blockchain transactions carry the assets from one node to another node and the assets are the essential data that may represent financial information, business information, healthcare information, or even personal information. Bitcoin is the most popularly used application of blockchain, which is essentially financial cryptocurrency. During the financial transactions, Bitcoin is being transferred between different individuals and is executed through blockchains. In addition to Bitcoin, another popular application of blockchain is Ethereum. It is stated in \[[3](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_3)\] that Etherium, “it has been created not only for implementation over the transactions, but contracts which are involved in the transactions with conditions and rules.” Such contracts are named smart contracts and have an application domain named the Internet of Things (IoT), where the bulk of devices work together and create smart contracts for exchanging data and processes with each other. Essentially, it is a point-to-point technology with no centralized controlled authority. Blockchains are specially designed to deal with secured transactions and transactions involving data \[[4](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_4), [5](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_5)\]. These transactions hold huge amounts of data that have to be collected securely, processed, analyzed, and shared with the various transactions involved in the blockchain network. That is, one of the popular data science techniques that are implemented in most of the transactions. Blockchains dispense a secure medium to run this transaction over the network. This chapter elaborates the blockchain technology and its implementation for enhancing the Cyber Security. [Section 4.2](#chapter4.xhtml#sec4_2) Discusses blockchain technologies, their features, and the type of the blockchain. [Section 4.3](#chapter4.xhtml#sec4_3) discusses the recent work. [Section 4.4](#chapter4.xhtml#sec4_4) Analysis of Cyber Attacks and Crimes as well as most common type of cybercrime and an analysis of cyber attack and crime in India and the United States (US) of five years is also discussed in this block. [Section 4.5](#chapter4.xhtml#sec4_5) contains the Blockchain Technology (BCT) strategy for Cyber Attacks and Crimes, [Section 4.6](#chapter4.xhtml#sec4_6) discusses how the BCT helps to predict criminal behavior and [Sections 4.7 and 4.8](#chapter4.xhtml#sec4_8) are the conclusion and the future work.

## 4.2 Blockchain Technologies

Blockchain is mainly an organized collection of blocks that are held together via chains. A block is mainly a file that holds the data content pertaining to a transaction. The data of the current block may be transferred to multiple other blocks known as nodes \[[6](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_6), [7](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_7)\]. Furthermore, a block may sustain data from numerous other blocks. The data content holded in each block is everlasting and immutable. Additional Blocks can be added to the blockchain during the transaction progresses. During the process each transition has to be verified well. However, unlike in non-blockchain applications the centralized authority will verify each and every transaction all the time, the same way in a blockchain based transaction, it is each and every time verified by a collection of process blocks distributed over the blockchain network \[[8](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_8)\]. As discussed in the NIST document \[[9](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_9)\], the blocks can be published in two ways with or without permissions, firstly with permissions where the blocks can be published only with the permission of an authority, either by centralized or by decentralized and secondly when there is a lack of authority for granting the permission in this scenario anyone can publish a block at any time. Blockchain technology also has an important component for implementing the security by using the concept of cryptographic hash functions. This is a form of a message digest where checksum is computed based on the data contents. This is one of the key components that provide data security along with data confidentiality, purity, integrity for blockchains. Another key component of blockchain network is the transaction which is the means to show the interactivity between two parties. Transactions are also the way to exchange the cryptocurrencies between the users of the blockchain. Blockchains mainly use asymmetric key encryption methodology which is essentially public key cryptography. It also uses address of network which is acquired from the public key cryptography. The decentralized ledger is the core of blockchain, i.e., a collection of transactions. The transactions are executed in a disseminate fashion, so the architecture which supports the blockchain as a distributed ledger. As the transactions are started executing, blocks get added one by one to the blockchain which contain information such as a list of validated transactions and the metadata about these transactions. These blocks are clumped together to form the blockchain. There are several other information details that pertain to blockchains such as the consensus model for forks and smart contracts, (also known as proof of work model). In this model, the work has been completed when user publishes next. This model is used by Bitcoin. In some cases, blockchains may need some changes, called forks. As stated in \[[9](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_9)\], On the blockchain network smart contract (collection of data and code) is deployed with cryptographically signed transactions and blockchain nodes executed smart contracts, e.g., Ethereum and Hyperledger Fabric's chaincode. The process to execute transactions using blockchain and details of different components found in \[[9](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_9), [10](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_10)\].

### 4.2.1 Features of Blockchain

The blockchain technology is gaining more and more popularity, not because it is used for transactions related to Bitcoin and cryptocurrency but because it has numerous extensive features that make it unique and secure for implementation. (1) Scalable and decentralized: blockchain is based on the feature of decentralization, here there is no central authority for regulating everything, instead the node blocks of the network work together to validate, verify, archive, store, and update the transactional data. (2) Transparent: information stored in each block is transparent for each and every node present in the blockchain network, it is also transparent for all when data is modified or created, that is why blockchain is trusted much. (3) Identity and access: each and every single block present over the network can be recognized by other participants using the same common blockchain architecture. The data generated by the nodes is shared to all the other members of the chain and it is verified uniquely by them. (4) Open source: majority of the time the blockchain frameworks are open to all, documenting can be publicly verified by any block of the chained network. (5) Autonomy: each and every node on the blockchain network can securely transfer or update information, the concept is to trust the entire system to form a single entity and no one can get involved in it. (6) Immutable: all the data records will be held indefinitely and cannot be modified unless at the particular time someone can take control of more than 51 percent node. (7) Anonymity: blockchain technologies handle the trust problem between nodes, so that the transaction as well as the data transfer can be anonymous, only needed to know the person's blockchain address. (8) Consensus model(s): the consensus model(s) help the chain network to maintain unbiased decisions. During the phase of decision making the group of active nodes can react to the agreement quickly and fast for enhancing the smooth functioning of the blockchain network.

### 4.2.2 Types of Blockchain

The blockchain technology is implemented for performing the secured network transactions as per the requirement. Blockchain can be implemented in three ways \[[11](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_11)\] like, **(1) Public blockchain:** Each and every node in the network can authenticate the transaction in this blockchain and can engage in the consensus-building process. Originally, the blockchain network was planned to securely cut down the central authority during an asset exchange scenario. It ensures decentralization by chaining up the blocks for peer-to-peer transactions. Each transaction is associated with the blockchain needed to be authenticated and synchronized with every node block in the network \[[11](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_11), [12](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_12)\]. Any machine with a proper internet connection can be enrolled as a block node and can gain the complete blockchain history. The distribution of records in public blockchain makes it extremely secure. However, transaction processing and verification by each node is a very slow and time-consuming process. The energy consumption required to validate each transaction is extreme and increases dramatically with every node that gets added to the network. (2) **Private blockchain:** It is the restricted type of blockchain that allows only limited nodes to participate and present in the network. This blockchains network follows a strict authenticity mechanism for data access in the network. None of the nodes will participate in the transaction without confirmation and validation. Due to small network size the verification and validation of the transactions are fast and with higher level of efficiency. Since there is only \[[11](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_11)\] a significant deficiency in this blockchains is that it does not provide decentralized security as provided by the public blockchains. (3) **Consortium blockchain:** This blockchain is a combined formation of public and private blockchains and can be called as partially decentralized. In this network, data as well as transaction details can either be open source or private in nature and the node has the authority to choose it in advance. It is difficult to realize the difference between a blockchain consortium and a fully private blockchain network. However, the discrepancy has not been examined in detail till now. In general, the consortium blockchain is a hybrid formation between a very less trusted public blockchain and the mostly trusted private blockchain entity model, while the private blockchains network can be specifically identified as conventional hierarchical networks with cryptographic verifications and attached validations.

## 4.3 Related Works

In \[[13](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_13)\] N. Kshetri et al. discuss the countermeasures of blockchain technology for cyber defense and cyber security in this chapter the author discusses the different types of cyber attacks and how the blockchain helps to counter their attacks with different properties applicable to remove these attacks. This article clearly elaborated the use of blockchain technology and its types with respect to cyber defense and cyber security and the solution for the challenges that commonly arise while implementing them and provide the solution to overcome the problem.

In \[[14](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_14)\] S. Sanober et al., this chapter highlighted the facts for increasing the numbers of cybercrime in day-to-day activities over the network, it is due to maximum utilization of network resources and connections in the past recent year. For detecting these types of malicious activities blockchain provides various factors for removal as well as prevention from the cyber attacks. In this chapter, the blockchain capability is enhanced by using the concept of principal component analysis methods, this helps in increasing the rate of detection as well as removal of the bad data from the blockchain network and also helps in fast identification of the threats from the network.

In \[[15](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_15)\] A. Razaque et al., this research chapter the author analyzes the fact that most of the people lack the cyber awareness, knowledge, guidelines and the rules and regulations regarding the internet uses they can easily get affected with the cyber threats. To overcome these problems and reduce the risk of cybercrime, a web-based blockchain-enabled cybersecurity awareness program (WBCA) is introduced which helps in improving the knowledge of the users toward the common behaviors and practices of the cybercriminals and enhance their security skills, modern cybersecurity vulnerabilities, and trends.

In \[[16](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_16)\] L. Yuchong et al. this research work highlights the increasing need and demand of the communication technology over different domains like economic, commercial, government, cultural, and social and also increases the chance of cyber attack that can harm the data shared over the network. The chapter objective is to find the challenges, weaknesses, and the strength of the cyber security over the cyber attacks occurring nowadays, its recent developments, and the challenges faced during the time of its implementation.

In \[[17](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_17)\] P.J. Taylor et al., the article mainly focuses on the features of the blockchain network like secured data storage as well as its transfer through decentralized, peer-to-peer systems. This chapter also highlights the implementation of blockchain for cybersecurity on different platforms like IoT, web-based applications, AI data, and secure data storage of personally identifiable information.

## 4.4 Analysis of Cyber Attacks and Crimes

We have seen a reminiscent growth in cyber attacks and crimes over the past years. Personal data leaked on a massive scale worldwide. Cyber criminals seek to make the most of safety vulnerabilities to steal passwords, records, or money \[[18](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_18), [19](#chapter4.xhtml#ref4_19)\]. Most of the familiar cyber threats comprise:

*   **Hacking** – this includes social media and email passwords.
    
*   **Phishing** – bogus emails that ask for security statistics and private data.
    
*   **Malicious software programs** – consisting of ransomwares through which criminals seize archives and preserve it for ransom.
    
*   **Distributed denial of carrier (DDOS) attacks against web sites** – frequently coincide with the aid of extortion.
    

The range and convolution of cyber attacks is huge belonging “Off the shelf” tools that means criminals who has less technical knowledge are now capable of committing cybercrime, and do so as recognition of the practicable income becomes more widespread.

### 4.4.1 Most Common Types of Cyber Attacks

Various kind of Cyber Attacks nowadays and from past year's observations, cybercriminal change their attacking strategy to harm individuals or organizations. On the basis of past years Cyber Attacks, the top 10 most similar types of cyber attacks which are listed in [Table 4.1](#chapter4.xhtml#tbl4_1) A graphical representation is in [Figure 4.1](#chapter4.xhtml#fig4_1).

[Table 4.1](#chapter4.xhtml#b4tbl4_1) Top 10 most common types of cyber attacks

  
| S.N. || Common type of cyber attack || Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **1** || Malware || 

*   It is the infected code attached with the legitimate code, and gets spreaded over the network. It can be in the form of viruses, worms, Trojans, spywares and ransomwares.
    
*   This infected code has the capability to access the private network, steal the sensitive data and interrupt the process execution.
    

 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **2** || Denial-of-Service (DoS) Attacks || 

*   This attack overruns the system resources so that it cannot fulfill the service request. Affected system is under the control of the attack launchers, they will hack the system resources. These attacks are in the form of TCP SYN flood attack, teardrop attack, ping-of-death attack, and smurf attack.
    

 |
| **3** || Phishing || 

*   This attack steals the person's credential and sensitive information by means of affected emails, hyperlinks in the form of attachments linked with the documents seem to be sent from the trusted sources.
    
*   Attack techniques include whaling, pharming, deceptive and spear phishing.
    

 |
| **4** || Spoofing || 

*   In this technique the attacker disguises themselves as an authentic and trusted source and steals confidential information, money and can infect the computer system by harmful software.
    
*   Spoofing is of different type like Domain spoofing, Email spoofing and ARP spoofing
    

 |
| **5** || Identity-Based Attacks || 

*   Cyber attackers steal the credential data of a person and create a clone of that person to access their personal, financial, organizational based information. The original user is not aware that his data is being leaked and by whom.
    
*   Attacks can occur in different ways: man-in-middle attack, credential stuffing and password spraying.
    

 |
| **6** || Code Injection Attacks || 

*   In this type of attack the attacker injects the malicious code into the application for getting it infected.
    
*   This attack mainly occurs due to lack of handling the unstructured data, poor implementation of data validation and lack of data management.
    

 |
| **7** || Supply Chain Attacks || 

*   This type of attack occurred when someone outside the organization gained access to the system with the help of an authorized person.
    
*   In this the outsider gains the access to manipulate the sensitive data, applications and networks settings.
    

 |
| **8** || Insider Threats || 

*   An insider is a person who has the authority to access, manipulate the organizational data for fulfilling the motive to harm the organization he leaks the data to the outside world.
    
*   The attack directly affects the integrity, confidentiality, and availability of the organization data, personnel, or facilities.
    

 |
| **9** || DNS Tunneling || 

*   This type of attack encodes data of the programs or protocols into the DNS queries and responses.
    
*   Data payloads that can be added to an attacked DNS server to control DNS server and applications includes DNS tunneling.
    

 |
| **10** || IoT-Based Attacks || 

*   In this cyber attack IoT devices helps to gain access of user sensitive data.
    
*   On the device Malware has been installed by attackers to harm the device, or acquire access to next credential data of company.
    

 |

![Figure 4.1 Top 10 most common types of cyber attacks.](images/fig4_1.jpg)

[Figure 4.1](#chapter4.xhtml#b4fig4_1) Top 10 most common types of cyber attacks.

Cyber Attacks tactics are shifting nowadays from businesses over individuals and also phishing attacks on individuals are continuously rising. It is because Cyber Criminals are far from attacking locations like from different states or countries.

## 4.5 BCT Strategy for Cyber Attacks and Crimes

Advancements in a variety of areas have been promised thanks to the advantages of digital transformation. However, the shift to digital platforms for company operations, governance procedures, and financial transactions has brought up cybersecurity issues. Globally, billions of people who use digital services are at danger of serious threats including phishing attacks, which compromise their personal and financial information. A blockchain solution for cybersecurity has the ability to offer users and companies a secure environment. By 2023, the annual cost of cybercrime might reach $8 trillion.

In the recent past, there have been numerous attacks on the data of different institutions, including Netflix, Twitter, and others, including the “wanna Cry ransomware” attack and other attacks, such as the theft of $81 million from the Bangladesh bank to stop these severe attacks against the nation's economy. Currently, blockchain is a better alternative to stop these kinds of actions because it is assisting organizations with monitoring.

Transaction verification processes are no longer dependent on a single central institution because of the digitization and distribution of record-keeping enabled by blockchain technology. Blockchains are always distributed, but their permissions, sizes, roles, transparency, participant kinds, and transaction processing methods vary greatly. Decentralization eliminates the single point of failure, which improves security by design.

Along with these additional security features, blockchains also include contracts, identity controls, public and private keys, software-mediated consensus, and cryptography. By confirming access, certifying transaction records, demonstrating traceability, and upholding privacy, these inherent properties provide data protection and integrity.

While the technology used to create blockchain-based platforms and applications have the potential to increase security, they never serve as the foundation. Prior to actively designing, testing, implementing, and managing new security capabilities, security professionals must first identify the issues, interactions, and trade-offs for such capabilities in collaboration with product and platform builders.

As we have discussed the mostly 10 similar nature of cyber attacks. Among these types of attacks most of them will breach the network to gain the access of user credentials. Most of the attackers want to breach the data of popular organizations, companies or individual famous personalities where their main motive is to gain more and more profit. The world become more digitized day by day so cyber attacks get increased, we cannot stop them but with some strategies and cure our data would less affected by cyber attacks as we know that Denial-of-service(Dos) and DNS tunneling attacks get reduced, if data managed with blockchain technology. So to prevent cyber attacks and crime, blockchain plays a vital role and it manages data in the blocks linked with chain helps to reduce these types of attacks.

Before discussing the blockchain strategy for cyber attacks and crimes, we should also cure below terms

1.  **Step 1**: Level 1 Prevention – No physical damage of machine, servers have backups in different locations, etc.
    
2.  **Step 2**: Level 2 Prevention – User authentication at every level
    
3.  **Step 3**: Level 3 Prevention – The software (hospital, any company or organization, etc.) must have proper validation (free from SQL injection attacks)
    
4.  **Step 4**: Level 4 Prevention – User's data (sensitive) saved in the form of blockchain
    

Although user credentials (data) is more sensitive and important so we have to secure the data from cyber attacks (Level 4) but we have to keep in mind the security of Level 1, Level 2, and Level 3 too. The BCT strategy for cyber attacks and crimes discussed in [Table 4.5](#chapter4.xhtml#tbl4_5).

[Table 4.5](#chapter4.xhtml#b4tbl4_5) BCT strategy for cyber attacks and crimes.

  
| Sr No. || BCT strategy for cyber attacks and crimes || Detail description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **1** || User's data(sensitive) not be centralized and also not be stored in original form || **Persistence and Accessibility:**

*   Decentralized environment supports persistence against attacks, corruption and downtime.
    
*   Decentralizing helps to reduce in the event of DDoS attacks.
    

 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| **2** || Alternation of data through the authorized user || **Data Integrity:**

*   Data on blockchains cannot be altered.
    
*   Each network node is cross referenced and requires consensus to verify transactions.
    

 |
| **3** || Easy to trace || **Traceability and Provenance:**

*   Traceability and transparency are the core of blockchain, but their security benefits manifest differently in different applications.
    
*   Blockchain also improves privacy and security of transactions through remittances and cross-border payments.
    

 |
| **4** || Licensed s/w or authentication of s/w must be necessary || **Authentication of software and/or device interactions:**

*   Blockchain hashing helps organizations to verify updates, downloads and patches with developers.
    
*   It also helps to prevent supply chain attacks.
    

 |
| **5** || Authentication of individual's || **Individual's Authentication:**

*   Blockchain stores data in chains with a hash instead of the user's personal information.
    
*   Company's use blockchain encryption and hashing to secure and protect data in chatting, messaging, etc.
    

 |

As discussed BCT strategies there must be diminishment in cyber attacks and crimes but lack of awareness of blockchain and its features and trust issues prevent organizations to upgrade their software's or develop in blockchain.

## 4.6 BCT Helps to Predict Criminal Behavior

BCT plays insignificant role in predicting the behavior of criminals. Cyber Criminals and others do crimes by following their set pattern of crime behavior. If criminal records are managed in the form of blockchains then we fetch out important criminal crime patterns attributes. All criminals’ data is stored in blocks and linked together. Each block contains information of criminals with basic attributes like criminal id, criminal name along with crime pattern like crime type, crime location, etc. [Figure 4.2](#chapter4.xhtml#fig4_2) demonstrates this.

## 4.7 Conclusion

In current digitized era where the number of digital transactions increased year by year; cyber attacks and crimes also increased in a disaster manner. Every organization/individual thinks about their data security. Malware, Denial of Service (DoS), and much more; are the most frequent types of cyber attacks through which attacker theft the organization's data, use it as they wanted, change or destroy it, and also gain financial profit. Data Security is the biggest challenge nowadays. So in this book chapter we discussed the blockchain technology and its features, how this technology secure the user credential and prevent from cyber attacks. Blockchain keeps data secure and altering of data is almost impossible because of its encryption and decentralized approach. We also discussed the 10 most common types of cyber attacks which affected the whole world and analyze the cyber attacks and crimes happens in five years of two countries – India and the US which as expected increases year by year and financial losses also increased. We proposed the strategy for cyber attacks and crime with the blockchain technology. Organizations have to keep their data's in a block chain form but due to lack of awareness, trust except Healthcare system and some organizations other sectors not use it. The cyber attacks and crime get reduced if data manages with BCT due to its powerful features. BCT also helps to predict criminal behavior. The block contains criminal basic information's with some additional crime patterns which help to analyze and prevent crime happen in future.

## 4.8 Future Scope

As we know blockchain technology keeps data secure and prevent from cyber attacks due to its strong features. The encryption (hash method) and decentralized approach helps to secure sensitive information of user and prevent from unauthorized access. In several businesses, blockchain has proven to be a legitimate disruptor. Transactions, internal communications, and data storage have all been efficiently optimized. Digital currencies are being created by international organizations using the technology. Additionally, it has aided businesses in enhancing data openness and reducing the likelihood of fraud. Customers can even access cloud-based services to host, create, and use blockchain applications.

Every day, there are millions of transactions in the finance sector. Cost-effectiveness, security, and transparencies are therefore the main priorities for the sector. The security of transactions has become a problem for the finance industry due to the rise in cybercrimes. The sector has benefited from the development of blockchain.

So the blockchain technology is not limited for only specific sector or domain. It implementation and secure aspects spread its limitation to many sectors like Banks, Healthcare, Insurances, and much more and perhaps it will be seen in upcoming years.
