# Chapter 5Blockchain Technology for Healthcare and Self-Sovereign Identity

Dilip Kumar

DOI: [10.1201/9781003449515-5](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781003449515-5)

## 5.1 Introduction

### 5.1.1 Blockchain

In the world of medicine, patient and prescription data must be classified to avoid a breeching privacy and then distribute to clinic and hospitals and able to organize all this data safely and securely. A modern solution has arisen in the form of blockchain technology.

Let's take a scenario, if we have two cities assuming City A and City B depicted in [Figure 5.1](#chapter5.xhtml#fig5_1). Now one person went to City A and the person having some medical history which get stored in the City A (refer to [Figure 5.2](#chapter5.xhtml#fig5_2)), usually in database of City A.

![Figure 5.1 Traditional data stores (City A and City B).](images/fig5_1.jpg)

[Figure 5.1](#chapter5.xhtml#b5fig5_1) Traditional data stores (City A and City B).

![Figure 5.2 Depict medical history store in City A.](images/fig5_2.jpg)

[Figure 5.2](#chapter5.xhtml#b5fig5_2) Depict medical history store in City A.

After this, the person switches to City B (refer to [Figure 5.3](#chapter5.xhtml#fig5_3)) due to some reasons and having medical issue again. Now in City B, the person may not get the data that was available in City A or database of City A.

![Figure 5.3 Patient in City B.](images/fig5_3.jpg)

[Figure 5.3](#chapter5.xhtml#b5fig5_3) Patient in City B.

So, this situation leads to the requirement of distributed database or distributed application (refer to [Figure 5.4](#chapter5.xhtml#fig5_4)) instead of standalone data store. There are many distributed applications and databases which may store and distribute data over the Internet but still there are some concerns that grow the requirement of the blockchain technology.

![Figure 5.4 Distributed solution or centralize data store.](images/fig5_4.jpg)

[Figure 5.4](#chapter5.xhtml#b5fig5_4) Distributed solution or centralize data store.

There are some issues in non-blockchain (standalone data store/databases or distributed applications) technologies that are mentioned in the following:

*   Data mistiness
    
*   Security problem (access controls, authentication, non-repudiation, and interoperability)
    
*   Centralized storage
    
*   Data sharing and access
    
*   Mutability
    

Therefore, to give the solution to these stated issues, we can use blockchain technology in healthcare, and with the characteristics of blockchain, we can made patient's data and prescription secure, transparent, and immutable.

The definition of a blockchain is that “A blockchain is a digital transaction ledger, maintained by a network of multiple computing machines that are not relying on a trusted third party” \[[1](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_1)\]. Nakamoto \[[2](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_2)\] stated that blockchain is the foundation of a novel payment system that will spark the development of cryptocurrencies after its launch in 2008. Due to its distinctive characteristics, the principle of blockchain has now been modified and then utilized in numerous additional circumstances \[[3](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_3)\]:

*   Spreads the responsibility for decision-making among numerous nodes or entities (distributed)
    
*   Agreement on the truth (immutable)
    
*   Working together between different organizational boundaries (transparent)
    
*   The use of tokens to help with direct value exchange (crypto-economic)
    

Blockchain is a decentralized, transparent, secure, and distributed digital ledger technology used to store transactions or information. Blockchain stores information in an immutable ledger, which is distributed and completely transparent. In blockchain, once data is get recorded, it becomes immutable. Immutable means recorded data is difficult to alter or delete. This is happening through cryptographic hashing and consensus mechanisms. Blockchain is a secure way to record or store information. Even we can say that it not only records information but also helps to track assets and build the trust also. Blockchain is a continuously growing collection of ordered entries, or blocks, that are put together to form a digital ledger. A cryptographically verified block comprising each transaction is then automatically authenticated by the network.

Blockchain record is being encapsulated in blocks and each block has its unique identification called hash. These blocks are connected, and each has the hash of the earlier block that is the reason for the name blockchain. This arrangement of blocks makes modification very difficult since all other blocks will be modified as a result so blockchain makes a great place to record information that we don't want to alter. In order to add a new information in block of blockchain or need to modify any recorded information, this alteration will send to every participant or node who are connected to this network to get validated. If more than 50% of connected participant are validating information out of all connected, then alteration will take place as valid information otherwise consider tempered data. For this process each node receives a reward for their work usually in the form of cryptocurrency. Blockchain enables a secure, immutable, and distributed database that can operate without a supervisor or central authority. In recent years, blockchain technology has been gaining a lot of attention as interest in a number of industries including healthcare has grown \[[4](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_4)\].

Here [Figure 5.5](#chapter5.xhtml#fig5_5) depicts more about how a blockchain functions in terms of cryptography. A chain of connected blocks, as suggested by the name “blockchain,” forms a blockchain. Once connected, these blocks cannot be modified or disconnected. They must be joined in a precise order. This fundamental idea underlies the immutability and security of the blockchain. The payload, or collection of transactions, and the block hash are the two primary elements of each block, as shown in [Figure 5.5](#chapter5.xhtml#fig5_5). The fundamental idea behind preserving the chain's immutability is strongly connected to the process used to create each block's hash \[[5](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_5)\]:

![Figure 5.5 How blockchain achieves data immutability and integrity. (Based on Rocha 2021.)](images/fig5_5.jpg)

[Figure 5.5](#chapter5.xhtml#b5fig5_5) How blockchain achieves data immutability and integrity. (Based on Rocha 2021.)

  
**h****a****s****h**(**x**) = **h****a****s****h**((**h****a****s****h**(**x**−1)+**p****a****y****l****o****a****d** (**x**))  

The hash of each block is determined based on the information within it and the block's hash before it. These designs have the immutability of record as a byproduct, which is exactly what we desired. According to logic, it is impossible to maintain the integrity of the cryptography problem throughout each modification in a block's record. If any malicious attempt is made to change historical data, as a result it destroys the link which links the previous block to the next block, leading to a malfunctioning chain and inaccurate records \[[6](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_6)–[9](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_9)\]. However, a malicious actor within the system might be able to change the one you want to block and make each chain's next block tenable. These advances move on to the next tier of protection within the decentralized model for blockchain. A blockchain does not have a central processing hub to process and validate the connection despite the fact that each exchange in the computer network is protected, regarded as secure and accurate. The only way this is feasible is using a consensus procedure, a crucial component of every blockchain system.

The consensus rules acting like a conduit for communication between all network peers, or “nodes,” come to an understanding regarding the status of the distributed ledger at any one time. By doing this, the protocol can build confidence among unidentified actors in the network. Fundamentally, the step-by-step instructions guarantee that every added link in the chain represents truth and has earned everyone's approval. Fortunately, a number of implementations are already available with advantages and disadvantages depending on the use case and application industry \[[10](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_10)\].

### 5.1.2 Why Blockchain?

An outline of the technological components makes blockchain possible to provide a considerable collection of benefits. This section will discuss the kind of benefits needed in cases like these. It is crucial to first discuss the variations and connections between blockchain and distributed ledger technology (DLT). These two names are frequently confused with one another or taken to mean the same thing. Despite their similarities and relationship, distributed ledger and blockchain are two entirely separate concepts. Although a blockchain might be viewed as a DLT, there are other DLTs as well.

Consequently, the term “distributed ledger technology” is used. A DLT is a database that is utilized and managed by numerous individuals across several validation nodes. In contract, a blockchain is capable of keeping track of transactions by utilizing an unchangeable secret signature known as a hash. Then, every record (which refers to database modifications) is categorized into blocks. The name “blockchain” refers to the connection between each block and the one before it, created by the hash of the earlier block.

### 5.1.3 Profitable Blockchain Platforms

#### 5.1.3.1 Ethereum

Ethereum is one of the most popular programmable blockchain technologies and well-known platforms and is likely the most popular choice. A virtual machine that utilizes Ethereum is called the Ethereum Virtual Machine (EVM). With the help of EVM, code can be validated and run on the blockchain with the assurance that it will function uniformly on every device. Smart contracts include this code, logical units which, depending on their programmable logic, are capable of autonomous behavior. They can exchange messages with other smart contracts or other blockchain users. Decentralized processing and confirmation are used to process and verify the smart contracts’ network, memory, and logic state. Ethash, a PoW-based method, is being used in the network to obtain consensus \[[5](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_5)\]. From a technical perspective, Ethereum is a tried-and-true solution with a number of operational decentralized applications. The Ethereum network that is now in use, however, has a number of scaling issues and is expensive \[[5](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_5)\]. However, it is also currently the largest visible blockchain.

#### 5.1.3.2 Hyperledger Sawtooth and Fabric

Enterprise-grade blockchain frameworks, tools, and libraries are under the umbrella of the Hyperledger project. It was initiated by the Linux Foundation, and companies including Intel, Airbus, IBM, J.P. Morgan, and many more have contributed to it. The primary goal is to create trustworthy and effective blockchains to enable multinational corporations. Different protocol and consensus techniques as well as various approaches to manage smart contracts are included in the various subprojects. Anyone can examine Hyperledger Sawtooth and Hyperledger Fabric for this situation.

Sawtooth is made to be adaptable and scalable. Both permissioned and permissionless installations are supported, so either all users can access or participate in the blockchain. Proof of Elapsed Time (PoET), a brand-new consensus algorithm, is included with its release. However, the consensus algorithm is interchangeable in this implementation. PoET, however, uses a small number of resources to target widely dispersed validator populations. Transaction families are created by abstracting away the business logic of transactions from the consensus layer. This architectural feature allows it to currently support a number of programming languages for implementation, C++, JavaScript, Python, Go, and Swift. Presently, support for Ethereum smart contracts is provided by Hyperledger Burrow integration. Presently, Sawtooth has a number of enterprise applications running across many different domains \[[5](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_5)\]. On the other hand, we additionally have Fabric from Hyperledger. Fabric only supports permissioned blockchains, whereas Sawtooth supports all blockchain types. Fabric also lacks the concept of transaction families. Ethereum contracts and smart contracts are very similar. Fabric presents a revolutionary smart contract execution architecture \[[5](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_5)\]. The bulk of existing smart-contract blockchain solutions feature an order-execute design, which requires every operation to be verified, specified, and delivered to all nodes before being executed by each node. This means that determinism is a requirement for smart contracts. Otherwise, it may be difficult to come to a consensus. Usually, a DSL forces this to take place.

[Figure 5.6](#chapter5.xhtml#fig5_6) illustrates a simple illustration of the deployment of a sawtooth network. There, the connections between the current node and other nodes that make up the distributed network as a whole are visible. In order to communicate with clients and act as a portal to the blockchain information, a node may additionally include a REST API. The fundamental component, where the logic specific to each SmartAgriChain field is located, is the transaction processor. It processes the logic required for our application and contains the rules for the system we have. The Sawtooth strategy enables several transaction processing units in the same network, enabling the usage of multiple applications. For instance, our network implementation might support other applications that use a different transaction processor but are running on the same blockchain.

#### 5.1.3.3 Other Considered Platforms

Another platform might be employed. Some of the other possibilities, however, are either not yet ready for use or are still in the early stages of development, such as Cardano, or they do not significantly outperform Hyperledger solutions, such as R3 Corda.

### 5.1.4 Background and Related Work

The idea used in this chapter defines the term and we also take a look at relevant work.

#### 5.1.4.1 Definition

The description that follows is intended to make key ideas clearer and to explain the study's goals and conclusions.

1.  **Electronic health records:** Hardin and Kotz \[[11](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_11), [12](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_12)\] define electronic health records (EHR) which refers as “patient-centered, real-time records that securely and immediately provides information accessible to authorized users.” It is a patient's medical history in digital form.
    
2.  **Interoperability:** According to the IEEE Standard Computer Dictionary \[[10](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_10)\], interoperability is “The capability of several systems or parts to share information and utilize the information that has been exchanged” \[[13](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_13)\].
    
3.  **Privacy:** The universal declaration of human rights mentions it \[[14](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_14)\]. Nobody shall be the target of arbitrary intrusion into their personal space, that of their family, their home, or their correspondence, as well as attacks on their honor and reputation. The notions of privacy as presented by Solove \[[15](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_15)\] are used in our study, and we adopt his methodology of analyzing privacy through its violations. The following categories are used in this taxonomy to group activities that violate privacy:
    
    *   **Information collection:** Independent parties gather knowledge by utilizing during an interrogation, there may be surveillance (recording, listening to, or watching), as well as questions or probing.
        
    *   **Information processing:** By aggregating (combining) the data, the records are preserved, utilized, and saved. Attributing the data to a certain individual (identification), lack of storage data protection (insecurity), secondary use, exclusion (keeping people from understanding what information is kept about them), and usage for purposes other to those for which it was originally intended.
        
    *   **Information dissemination:** The data owners distribute or share the information in a way that compromises confidentiality in some manner (breaking the confidentiality agreement for the information), disclosure (public disclosure of an individual's information), exposure (similar to disclosure but involving tangible things like nudity), improved access (facilitating information exposure and accessible), and extortion (threatening to disclose the details).
        
    *   **Invasion:** Independent individuals violate someone's privacy by performing invasive acts or interference with decisions (involves the government interfering with people's personal choices or can say private decisions).

## 5.2 Self-Sovereign Identity

Self-sovereign identity is a lifelong transferrable identity for any person, group, or item that is independent of any centralized power and that cannot be taken away.

As a requirement for a new foundational framework, which we refer to as self-sovereign identity in a nutshell, SSI refers to the idea that people or organizations have complete ownership of their analog and digital identities as well as the ability to regulate how their individual information is shared and utilized. The user is crucial for managing one's own identity. This offers a degree of flexibility and security that enables the identity holder to only divulge the information required for any specific contact or transaction.

Giving people or organizations the power to manage identity information emphasizes that identities are about much more than just signing in, as it appears that this explanation is a bit unclear.

Considering the value of the studies and applications considered throughout the survey's development \[[21](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_21)\], the characteristics (see [Figure 5.8](#chapter5.xhtml#fig5_8)) have been compiled. Timestamped unchanged data history, decentralized verification, self-executing smart contracts, transparency, interoperability, a decentralized value transfer system, and gamification are some of the key BC characteristics of these apps \[[21](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_21)\].

![Figure 5.8 BC characteristics in healthcare and use cases. (Based on Bahar Houtan 2020.)](images/fig5_8.jpg)

[Figure 5.8](#chapter5.xhtml#b5fig5_8) BC characteristics in healthcare and use cases. (Based on Bahar Houtan 2020.)

### 5.2.1 Principle to Follow by Self-Sovereign

Ten principles that self-sovereign identities must follow to guarantee individuals own their identities:

1.  **Existence:** Users must be self-sustaining.
    
2.  **Control:** Users must be able to maintain identification control.
    
3.  **Access:** Users’ identities and claims must always be accessible.
    
4.  **Transparency:** Open and transparent systems and methods must be utilized to manage identities.
    
5.  **Persistence:** Long-lasting user identities and claims.
    
6.  **Portability:** Identity-related data and services must be easily carried from one to another.
    
7.  **Interoperability:** User identities and claims ought to be adaptable across various industries, businesses, and even nations.
    
8.  **Consent:** A user must accept that their identities and claims may be used and disclosed.
    
9.  **Minimization:** In order to preserve privacy, it is important to limit the disclosure of identities and claims.
    
10.  **Protection:** The user's rights must be protected.
     

### 5.2.2 A Fundamental Idea of Several Types of Writing Identity

**The trust triangle:** There are three significant stakeholders (shown in [Figure 5.9](#chapter5.xhtml#fig5_9)) in this scenario, and the trust triangle outlines how a credential is granted and utilized.

![Figure 5.9 Depicts the three major stakeholders.](images/fig5_9.jpg)

[Figure 5.9](#chapter5.xhtml#b5fig5_9) Depicts the three major stakeholders.

1.  **Holder:** A subject, who holds a certain attribute or credential.
    
2.  **Issuer:** The identity information concern
    
3.  **Verifier:** Who requested the information.
    

If we want to prove our identity for example for opening a bank account, we need to authenticate ourselves with government ID. This ID is issued by the government to us as digital credential which is signed by the government which makes it a verified credential. We can then show a proof of this and verify credential to a verifier which in this case is the bank. Since the bank trusts the government, we have established a trusted relationship between all participants before diving more into the subject. I'd want to define an attribute. An attribute describes a person's qualifications without necessarily being specific to that individual.

These include parameters like height, weight, and gender.

A validated document produced by a third party, such as proof of citizenship, a college degree, or membership status, can likewise serve as an attribute. We might ask themselves, “Where does the data for the credential start?”. Although we do not yet have a single response, the SSI community has decided that this data should not be stored on a blockchain. Only the decentralized identities (DIDs) and short as well as, if required, a signature are stored on a blockchain. On a decentralized data storage, identification details data itself are maintained.

In these distributed identifiers, a real-world identification with regard to a specific use case or domain that has legal and practical ramifications, such as a phone number, a passport number, or an email address, is referred to by the term “DID” as an identifier.

Write digital identity because a decentralized identifier acts as a marker for a verified self. Because they are registered with distributed ledger technology or other forms of these centralized networks like blockchains, D IDs are completely under the control of the D ID holder and independent from any centralized registry identity provider or certificate authority. They are essentially a global unique identifier.

Self-sovereign identification (SSI) is a decentralized identity concept that enables people to be in charge of their own digital identities and private information without the need for centralized institutions or middlemen. Numerous industries use SSI for a variety of purposes.

### 5.2.3 Uses for Self-Sovereign Identity

*   **Digital identity verification:** With the help of SSI, people can securely and privately prove their identities online. It is beneficial for tasks like creating bank accounts, submitting job applications, or utilizing government services.
    
*   **Access control and permissions:** Through SSI, people may manage who has access to their personal data. Data privacy and security are ensured by users’ ability to grant and revoke rights for particular individuals or organizations to access their data.
    
    Healthcare: Patients can preserve control over their sensitive data while managing their health records and sharing particular medical information with healthcare providers or researchers.
    
*   **Education:** With the help of SSI, you may securely manage and distribute educational credentials like degrees and certificates. For companies and educational institutions, this makes the verification process simpler.
    
*   **Banking services:** In the banking sector, SSI can improve security and streamline procedures. By utilizing it for KYC (Know Your Customer) procedures, fraud and identity theft can be decreased.
    
    Passports and other travel documents can be stored as self-sovereign identities, facilitating safer and more efficient foreign travel.
    
*   **Voting and elections:** SSI can be utilized for transparent and secure online voting, lowering the possibility of voter fraud, and preserving the integrity of elections.
    
*   **IoT (Internet of Things):** IoT ecosystem devices can have their own autonomous identities, enabling secure communication with other IoT ecosystem devices and services.
    
*   **Government services:** Using self-sovereign identities, citizens can access government services, file tax returns, and receive benefits, increasing efficiency and lowering administrative costs.
    
*   **Supply chain and product authentication:** SSI can be used to track the movement of items across the supply chain and verify their authenticity, which helps to prevent counterfeiting and ensures product quality.
    
*   **Decentralized finance (DeFi):** Users can control their financial identities while managing their DeFi assets and interacting with decentralized applications (DApps).
    
*   **Online services and social media:** SSI can increase user privacy and provide individuals more control over their online accounts and data, lowering data breaches. Donations made for philanthropic purposes can be tracked and verified using SSI, ensuring accountability and transparency throughout the gift process.
    
*   **Legal and notary services:** By facilitating secure document signing, contract management, and notarization, SSI can speed legal processes.
    
    Humanitarian assistance and refugee support: SSI can give displaced people and refugees safe digital identities, enhancing access to necessities and help.
    

## 5.3 Blockchain in Healthcare

Blockchain is a system that can use for storing medical records and ensuring the authenticity of records and their integrity without the demand for a central authority or intermediary. It improves stability and demonstrates its vulnerability to hacking. If the records need to be transferred to a different area of the hospital or switch between doctors or transfer to another hospital setting up a unified electronic medical record, it gives the facility to share medical records among the different individual or group (as in [Figure 5.10](#chapter5.xhtml#fig5_10)). In addition, a number of use cases, such as retaining access rights in electronic health records (EHR) and simplifying claim processing, have demonstrated blockchain's promise as a platform for enhancing the veracity and openness of healthcare data \[[12](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_12)\].

![Figure 5.10 The depiction of a block in the blockchain (City B) and the execution of a transaction in the blockchain (City A).](images/fig5_10.jpg)

[Figure 5.10](#chapter5.xhtml#b5fig5_10) The depiction of a block in the blockchain (City B) and the execution of a transaction in the blockchain (City A).

### 5.3.1 Immutable Transaction Ledger

The Bitcoin cryptocurrency's infrastructure was the original purpose of the blockchain. The transfers of specific Bitcoin amount from one account to another are represented by the transactions on the blockchain of Bitcoin. By looking at the transactions on the public blockchain with the right software tools, anyone may determine which account a specific Bitcoin belongs to.

Transactions in the healthcare industry would be the documentation of certain events of healthcare services rendered. Patients, healthcare payers, and providers of healthcare would all contribute encrypted data with a patient ID to a public blockchain.

Clinical information that is now maintained in EHR systems, payer claim histories and gaps in care, patient device readings, and family history might all be included in this. Number of people affected by breaches of health information would be encrypted and kept in the blockchain, and only people with the patient's private key would be able to decrypt it ([Figure 5.11](#chapter5.xhtml#fig5_11)).

![Figure 5.11 Example of financial versus healthcare blockchain transactions. (Based on Drew Ivan 2016).](images/fig5_11.jpg)

[Figure 5.11](#chapter5.xhtml#b5fig5_11) Example of financial versus healthcare blockchain transactions. (Based on Drew Ivan 2016).

The ledger cannot be altered or deleted because it is unchangeable. Metadata information indicating the date, time, place, and entity making the modification is included in updates. A blockchain-based medical record will be self-auditing in this way (as in [Figure 5.12](#chapter5.xhtml#fig5_12)).

![Figure 5.12 Working steps of blockchain technology. (Based on Abid Haleem 2021.)](images/fig5_12.jpg)

[Figure 5.12](#chapter5.xhtml#b5fig5_12) Working steps of blockchain technology. (Based on Abid Haleem 2021.)

For different digital transactions, blockchain technology may be integrated to create a reliable, decentralized, and immutable ledger that performs better than the current methods, strategies, and procedures \[[19](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_19)\]. Additionally, the health system landscape is shifting toward a patient-centered strategy focusing on two key components: readily available services and always-appropriate healthcare resources \[[17](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_17)\].

The primary uses of blockchain technology in the healthcare industry include:

1.  **Electronic health records (EHRs):** Storing patient records and medical histories securely on a blockchain to assure data integrity, privacy, and accessibility are the main uses of blockchain technology in healthcare.
    
2.  **Drug traceability:** Keeping an eye on the supply chain in the pharmaceutical business to prevent the introduction of counterfeit drugs.
    
3.  **Clinical trials:** Ensuring the data is transparent and unchangeable.
    
4.  **Patient consent and identity verification:** Securing the management of patient consent forms and identity checks.
    
5.  **Healthcare supply chain:** Keeping an eye on and improving the flow of drugs and medical supplies. Facilitating safe contact between patients and medical professionals through telemedicine and remote patient monitoring.
    

### 5.3.2 Benefits of Blockchain in Healthcare

*   Healthcare firms were able to deliver proper patient care and top-notch medical services thanks to the blockchain.
    
*   Exchanging health-related information is also time-consuming activity and repeatable in nature which leads the costs in healthcare industry. Blockchain is again shining here.
    
*   Citizens may take part in initiatives for health education using blockchain technology.
    
*   Additionally, improved public well-being research and data sharing will improve care for many groups.
    
*   Blockchain can be combined with the most powerful and impactful technologies and can be used for research purpose and beneficial for mankind (as in [Figure 5.13](#chapter5.xhtml#fig5_13)). Blockchain ensures the security, transparency, traceability, and many more benefits for us.

### 5.3.3 How Blockchain Meets Healthcare Requirements

Here is high-level diagram clearly showing how blockchain meets the requirement in healthcare sector using [Figure 5.14](#chapter5.xhtml#fig5_14). This figure also shows some important application of blockchain.

1.  **Data sharing:** Data sharing is a basic necessity in the medical care industry in light of multiple factors:
    
    *   **Coordinated patient care:** Successful medical services conveyance frequently requires joint effort among numerous suppliers, including essential consideration doctors, subject matter experts, clinics, laboratories, and pharmacies. Information sharing empowers these partners to get to significant patient data as quickly as possible, prompting more organized and comprehensive care.
        
    *   **Clinical decision-making:** Admittance to thorough patient information, including clinical history, test results, and treatment plans, upgrades clinical decision-making and works on understanding results. Healthcare industries can take more informed decision about diagnoses, therapies, and care plans when they approach a total image of a patient's health status.
        
    *   **Efficiency and cost-effectiveness:** Sharing patient information electronically omits the dependency of paper-based records and manual processes, which prompts more noteworthy proficiency and cost adequacy in medical care. It limits duplicate tests and procedures, protects from medical blunders, and reduces administrative and clerk overhead to manage paperwork.
        
2.  **Data access:** Data access in the medical care industry is a central prerequisite that includes key viewpoints:
    
    *   **Patient-driven access:** Patients reserve the privilege to get to their own health data, including clinical records, test results, and treatment histories. Giving patients simple and secure access to their health information enables them to play a functioning job in dealing with their medical care and settling on informed conclusions about their therapy choices or treatment.
        
    *   **Healthcare provider access:** Healthcare providers, including doctors, attendants, and different clinicians, require ideal convenient patient information to convey great consideration. To take informed clinical decisions, coordinate across different specialties and ensure regularity in care of patients.
        
3.  **Security:**
    
    *   **Data privacy:** Data privacy is important to protect patient healthcare information. This information must be accessed by authorized persons for legitimate purpose. It covers access controls, user authentication, encryption, etc.
        
    *   **Data integrity:** Data integrity refers to preventing unauthorized modifications or tampering healthcare records to maintain their accuracy and reliability. To prevent data integrity, healthcare organization should use techniques like data validation and digital signature. Blockchain technology can also contribute more to maintain data integrity.
        
    *   **Data confidentiality:** To achieve data confidentiality, healthcare organizations must have policies and procedures to handling sensitive data. Healthcare organization can use encryption techniques, secure transmission using HTTPs for exchanging data over Internet, and implement access control.
        
4.  **Interoperable data exchange:** Interoperability is significant for empowering consistent information exchange between various medical services associations, systems, and partners. To secure and efficient data exchange of patient, standardized data formats and set rules can be used across different organizations to improve patient persistent results.
    

## 5.4 Conclusion and Future Scope

Blockchain is a strong and widely used technology today. It was first developed to store and safeguard digital data or currencies, although it is not confined to these only. It can have numerous potential applications in the field of healthcare (including healthcare) which are made feasible by the blockchain technology's enabling platform \[[24](#chapter5.xhtml#ref5_24)\]. Here, we'll concentrate on a variety of factors, such as immutability and integrity, blockchain technology, and distributed ledger technology. Because of industrial regulations, automation processes, paperless initiatives, privacy and security risk mitigation, and the need to improve patient health and wellness, academic research is gravitating toward these themes and examining new technologies and strategies to address them. An exhaustive analysis of the blockchain and self-sovereign identity has been conducted, and blockchain systems have been researched in depth. By thoroughly examining important papers pertaining to SSI and blockchain technologies, our study made a significant contribution to this discussion. An overview of the findings is given below.

In the **first segment**, we introduced and covered blockchain, its immutability and integrity, as well as its need. Here, we also list the profitable blockchain-based platforms and show how a sawtooth network may be deployed. The background information, associated research, and privacy concerns with electronic health records are explained **in the second part**. Numerous studies discuss healthcare and claim that blockchain-based technology has a wide range of potential applications in the medical sector. These studies also claim that there is still more research to be done before large-scale production in order to assess the technology's scalability, security, and affordability. In conclusion while choosing among ECG monitoring systems that fulfill their monitoring demands, match their usage contexts, and help with the fundamental requirements for disease monitoring can be difficult for researchers, doctors, and other professionals. In the **third segment**, we briefly covered the self-sovereign identity and the principle that follows from being self-sovereign and also incorporated the core principles of many forms of writing identity. We addressed blockchain in healthcare in the **fourth section** and contrasted financial and healthcare blockchain transactions.

The main applications of blockchain technology in the healthcare sector are also covered in this section. The discussion also focused on the benefits of blockchain in healthcare and featured some cutting-edge technology that can be used in conjunction with blockchain in the future. Blockchain is not limited to digital currency and healthcare, even it can be used in supply chain and logistics, finance and banking, healthcare, real estate, government and public service, education, energy, retail and e-commerce, and legal and governance.

## 6.1 Introduction

The application of technique and technology, process, and control protects systems, programs, devices, networks, and data from cyberattacks. Cyber data refers to that data stored in cyberspace. It may include the cloud storage or data in any server that may be accessed through the network. Cyber defense is the defensive mechanism or design technique for the safety of information from cyberattacks. Blockchain technology can be used to secure cyber data through the distributed database in cyberspace. The technique of protecting internet-connected systems such as servers, personal computers, mobile devices, electronic systems, networks, and data from malicious attacks is known as cybersecurity. Generally, cybersecurity consists of two words: cyber and security. Cyber refers to the technology used in cyberspace and security means technique and the way of process to secure the cyber data in cyberspace.

“Blockchain technology basically offers a platform for the cozy transfer of the records which are part of any transactions together with financial transactions and contracts. On the coronary heart of blockchain is cryptography that ensures that the data being transferred is not tampered with and provides authenticity and integrity” \[[1](#chapter6.xhtml#ref6_1)\]. Blockchain technology provides the notion of transaction interaction between two parties to provide security for the data.

“Blockchain era is one of the strategies that has the opportunity to decorate decentralization, transparency, equality, and obligation on the net” \[[2](#chapter6.xhtml#ref6_2)\]. Using blockchain can provide strong security to cyber data and applications as compared to stored in a central database. Blockchain technology uses the distributed concept and technology to implement security for securing data stored in cyberspace. The decentralized concept is the switch of control of an interest or organization to several local offices or government in place of one single. Blockchain uses a decentralized concept for executing the security mechanism to implement the secure application.

The use of blockchain in cybersecurity means there is no control on central one-unit control for the data and security mechanisms. The blockchain anatomy consists of block of three elements as described in [Table 6.1](#chapter6.xhtml#tbl6_1).

[Table 6.1](#chapter6.xhtml#b6tbl6_1) Elements of blockchain anatomy

  
| S. no. || Elements || Description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 || Data || The sender, receiver, and amount traded |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2 || Hash || It is the unique code of every node |
| 3 || Hash of the previous node || Every block is attached to one another making the chain |

It is a decentralized network of computers. Different blocks of blockchain execute in different network-connected distributed environments so there is no chance of decision-making by one individual or group's hand.

Our book chapter is organized in the following sections as follows: [Section 6.2](#chapter6.xhtml#sec6_2), which highlights the increasing use of the internet and the previous background for blockchain technology in cyberspace. [Section 6.3](#chapter6.xhtml#sec6_3) emphasized a need for blockchain technology in the cyber world due to the powerful features, properties, and attributes. The next section is [Section 6.4](#chapter6.xhtml#sec6_4) which describes the communication of data on the web and how blockchain can benefit and secure data communication. [Section 6.5](#chapter6.xhtml#sec6_5) explains the underlying characteristics of blockchain technology that provide a strong foundation for cyber defense and data security. The next section of our book chapter is [Section 6.6](#chapter6.xhtml#sec6_6) that discusses and offers the prospect of decentralization of computer networking and cryptography that forms the backbone for cybersecurity. The last section of our book chapter is [Section 6.7](#chapter6.xhtml#sec6_7) where we conclude about the nature of the blockchain network that ensures secure data and the future scope of blockchain technology that will impact several other fields of social sciences other than cybersecurity, cyber data, and cyber communication.

## 6.2 Today's Cyber World and Background

The latest global is a global of electronic media and growing use of the internet, in the main growing social websites lead to negative as well as superb effects in each human existence. Lifestyles in this era have ended up far greater comfy because of diverse virtual structures and the internet to help them. There's a turning aspect to the entirety right, and that also applies to the virtual world today. The net has added a nice change in our lives today; however, with that, there's additionally an enormous project in defending your facts. This gives upward thrust to cyberattacks. Most people spend their time on social websites intended to damage so there is no proper technology to screen activities.

Cyberattacks have numerous terrible consequences; they may cause statistics breaches, resulting in records loss or records manipulation. Businesses incur monetary losses, customer trust is hampered, and there is reputational harm. To minimize cyberattacks, we want to enforce cybersecurity to comfort the cyber statistics. Cybersecurity is the technique of safeguarding networks, PC structures, and their components from unauthorized virtual access. The use of allotted audit and tracking security by enforcing blockchain era may signal the occasion in cyberspace to decrease the cyber assaults.

The name blockchain comes from the fact that each transaction is recorded as a “block” of statistics. A block may file one or more information sorts, inclusive of quantity, price, or location. “The central thoughts in the back of blockchain technology emerged in the late Eighties and early Nineteen Nineties. In 1989, Leslie Lamport evolved the Paxos protocol, and in 1990 submitted the paper, The component Time Parliament” \[[3](#chapter6.xhtml#ref6_3)\]. Today's cyber world is the vast mode of distributed data in cyberspace. They need the mode of agreement to secure the data and to implement the security techniques to ensure the data security that can only be fulfilled by blockchain technology. Not only in the security concepts, it has already been used in cryptocurrency like bitcoin technology.

The trust on the blockchain network is enabled by four key characteristics of blockchain technology, described in [Table 6.2](#chapter6.xhtml#tbl6_2).

[Table 6.2](#chapter6.xhtml#b6tbl6_2) Characteristics of blockchain technology that enables trust

  
| **Ledger** || The era makes use of an append handiest ledger to offer complete transaction history. Not like conventional databases, transaction values in a blockchain are not overridden. |
| --- | --- |
| **Secure** || Blockchains are cryptographically comfy, ensuring that facts contained in the ledger have now not been tam. |
| **Shared** || The ledger is shared by multiple participants, providing transparency among the node members within the blockchain network. |
| **Distributed** || The blockchain can be distributed, allowing for the scaling of the number of nodes in a blockchain network to make it more resilient to attacks by malicious actors. |

“An important factor of blockchain technology is the use of cryptographic hash capabilities for plenty of operations. Hashing is a technique of applying a cryptographic hash feature to data, which calculates a surprisingly specific output (known as a message digest, or simply digest) for an input of nearly any length (e.g., a report, text, or picture). It permits people to independently enter records, hash that records, and derive the identical result – proving that there has been no exchange within the data” \[[3](#chapter6.xhtml#ref6_3)\].

## 6.3 Need of Blockchain Technology

The blockchain generation is used to provide security. “Blockchain technology has transformed the modern internet from ‘The internet of information sharing’ to ‘The internet of value exchange’” \[[2](#chapter6.xhtml#ref6_2)\].

Cybersecurity is enhanced by blockchain technology due to its decentralized nature and adherence to standards of protection, privacy, and security. Utilizing features such as transparency, cost-effectiveness, and heightened safety, it facilitates swift data processing. The real-time addition of records to a blockchain network is advantageous to anyone seeking to track assets and monitor transactions from start to finish, including invoices, orders, and payments.

Primarily based platforms permit individuals to directly exchange statistics while maintaining control over their personal records’ privacy and safety. Blockchain technology is needed for cyber safety, cyber statistics, and cyber communication for following motives.

Blockchain facilitates secure and transparent data sharing among multiple network devices in allocated strategies. Instead of depending on centralized servers, blockchain-based platforms allow individuals to directly exchange information while retaining control over their personal data privacy and security. Blockchain technology is essential for cybersecurity, cyber data, and cyber communication (as shown in [Figure 6.1](#chapter6.xhtml#fig6_1)) for the following reasons:

![Figure 6.1 Blockchain technology need for cybersecurity, cyber data, and cyber communication.](images/fig6_1.jpg)

[Figure 6.1](#chapter6.xhtml#b6fig6_1) Blockchain technology need for cybersecurity, cyber data, and cyber communication.

1.  **Transparency:** Blockchain generation can offer an obvious gadget in which all sports are traceable and seen to the public, wherein it can be used to display the motion of security and guarantee their authenticity which increases duty and acceptance as true with. The information in blockchain is distributed throughout a community of linked computer structures, making it extra at ease and less susceptible to unauthorized information tampering. Blockchain keeps complete records of beyond transactions inside the community, which means that the consumer can listen the records with complete transparency. Blockchain is predicated on consensus protocols across a community of nodes to verify transactions done in the community.
    
2.  **Security:** Blockchain relies on encryption methods to protect data and prevent tampering. It combines distributed agreement and cryptographic techniques, using advanced cryptographic algorithms to secure information and transactions. Each transaction is encrypted, and participants hold unique cryptographic keys for access to their data, ensuring confidentiality and integrity. The decentralized distributed ledger system records transactions across every network node, making it challenging for cybercriminals to view, steal, or alter data. Its design aims to create trust among participants.
    
3.  **Data Sharing:** A main component of blockchain technology is data exchange; all the nodes in blockchain network have access to the same data set. It is now a very effective and safe method to share data between numerous devices without central authority. Blockchain enables sharing across different organizations and networks by creating a common platform that connects them by allowing data to be exchanged and transferred among various parties without compromising security or transparency.
    
    Blockchain also allows data to be shared in real-time or near-real-time, enabling faster and more accurate decision-making.
    
4.  **Digital Identity:** Blockchain technology is used to generate virtual identity, which includes digital credentials and attributes. This platform is secure and only accessible to permissioned network participants. All actions and business processes are recorded as tamper-proof blockchain transactions, ensuring auditable, traceable, and verifiable identity information. Users can manage their own profiles and control information sharing, while issuers can easily connect with others and provide instant verification of credentials. This platform is anchored in trust and empowers all participants.
    

The three popular types of blockchain are public blockchain, private blockchain, and hybrid blockchain (see [Table 6.3](#chapter6.xhtml#tbl6_3)). All of the types have their own advantages and disadvantages with consortium blockchain as the fourth type. Characterized by shared responsibilities and collective governance, a unique structure is offered by consortium blockchain (the fourth type), which connects both public and private blockchains.

[Table 6.3](#chapter6.xhtml#b6tbl6_3) Types of blockchain with advantages, disadvantages, use cases, programming language, peer mode, and cryptocurrency \[4, 5\]

  
|  || Public blockchain || Private blockchain || Hybrid blockchain |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Definition** || Permissionless distributed ledger system open for anyone || Subset that operates within restricted environments || Innovation by merging private and public blockchains |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| **Cryptocurrency** || Bitcoin (built-in) || No built-in || Ether (built-in) |
| **Use cases** || Voting systems, auditable chains, finance, secure records || Supply chain management, asset verification, business || Real estate, finance, data security, industry requirements |
| **Programming language** || C++ || Golang || Solidity |
| **Peer mode** || Permissionless blockchain (only crypto) || Permissioned (only business) || Mixed (crypto + business) |
| **Advantages** || Trust and security, expansive network size, transparency, and openness as significant factor || Transactional speed, highly scalable, enhanced privacy, and data control for sensitive finance || Enhanced security, ensures privacy, cost-effective solution |
| **Disadvantages** || Transaction speed is low, intensive verification processes, high energy consumption, economic concerns || Trust building issues, centralization aspect against decentralization ethos, lack of independent audit || Lacks transparency, challenge of system upgrading, user incentives lacking to contribute |

## 6.4 Blockchain Technology for Cyber Communication

Communication refers to the transfer of cyber data from one device to another. Blockchain technology can be utilized to prevent information breaches, identity theft, cyberattacks, or criminal acts in cyber communication. Blockchain ensures that data remains private and secure. It can indeed enhance cybersecurity by serving as a platform for preventing fraudulent activities through consensus mechanisms. Blockchain can also detect data falsification through its inherent features such as operational resilience, data encryption, auditability, transparency, and immutability.

One of the main benefits of using blockchain technology in communication is its decentralized architecture. This means that there is no single point of control with the central server, making it more difficult for hackers to attack and compromise the system. Each node in the network holds a copy of the blockchain ledger, and any change in the ledger requires consensus from the network, making it nearly impossible to alter records on the blockchain without detection, enabling secure communication. Blockchain technology can be used to create a secure and decentralized data storage system. Instead of relying on centralized servers that are vulnerable to attack, data can be stored on a blockchain, which is tamper-proof and resistant to attack.

Smart home integration involves using wireless communications to control electronic devices, providing residents with complete control over their home's technology. These advancements enable remote management, increasing convenience and saving time. However, security concerns may arise due to potential loopholes or human errors, posing a risk to the safety credentials and overall security of the system \[[6](#chapter6.xhtml#ref6_6)\].

## 6.5 Blockchain Technology for Cyber Data

The underlying characteristics of blockchain technology of decentralization, consensus mechanism, immutability, traceability, and privacy provide a strong foundation for cyber defense and security of data and information in the cyberspace \[[7](#chapter6.xhtml#ref6_7)\]. Blockchain is a distributed database based on cryptography that maintains a constantly expanding list of data entries verified by every network node. Cyber defense aims to reduce possible attacks and understand the critical and sensitive information by carrying out technical analyses to find out possible paths.

Developments in recent years of blockchain in the cyberspace and gaming industries continue to run parallel leading to exceptional integration \[[4](#chapter6.xhtml#ref6_4)\]. Blockchain is well suited for cyber as a decentralized, financial solution for many reasons like digital proof of ownership, digital collectability, transferable value, corporate governance, accessibility, and interoperability. The measure of security over cyber and trust represented is paramount when turning to infrastructure and cybersecurity considerations for cyber communication, and cyber data (see [Figure 6.2](#chapter6.xhtml#fig6_2)).

![Figure 6.2 Blockchain technology (BCT) properties for cyber data.](images/fig6_2.jpg)

[Figure 6.2](#chapter6.xhtml#b6fig6_2) Blockchain technology (BCT) properties for cyber data.

Consensus mechanism is the protocol that determines how the community agrees on ledger modifications. Distributed ledger technology (DLT) refers to the protocols and infrastructure that enable computers in different locations to propose and validate transactions and update public records in a synchronized manner across a network \[[8](#chapter6.xhtml#ref6_8)\]. Delivery of services to the citizens to promote a better living standard is the main purpose of public service organizations in any nation. Blockchain technology has the capability to enable transparency and build citizens’ trust in public service delivery. Blockchain allows networked data transactions to be disseminated either publicly or privately to all users, enabling the secure and reliable archiving of any data format type.

The technology behind the world's common cryptocurrency, i.e., bitcoin is blockchain technology. Blockchain technology is grounded in peer-to-peer architecture in dissimilarity to the client-server architecture, where the power or ability doesn't relax on a single computer rather than the power rests on all the contestants of the network \[[9](#chapter6.xhtml#ref6_9)\]. Blockchain technology uses popular technical advancements like hashing, digital signatures, consensus mechanisms, and difficulty correction that will be helpful for cyber data. The blockchain system is maintained by a network of nodes, which are entities that do not have a pre-existing trust connection and are linked via a peer-to-peer network.

## 6.6 Blockchain Technology for Cybersecurity

Blockchain technology has emerged as a ground-breaking technology with several possible solutions to critical applications, from cybersecurity and supply chain management to the internet of medical things \[[10](#chapter6.xhtml#ref6_10)\]. Blockchain comprises blocks of data (transactions) that are arranged in linear sequence and accomplished on rare public services like online voting, cyber defense, and countermeasures. Inefficiency of civic service is contributed by drought of accountability, transparency, and trust. Blockchain technology has no doubt that it forms the backbone of cryptocurrency and now, cybersecurity (see [Figure 6.3](#chapter6.xhtml#fig6_3)).

![Figure 6.3 Blockchain technology properties for cybersecurity.](images/fig6_3.jpg)

[Figure 6.3](#chapter6.xhtml#b6fig6_3) Blockchain technology properties for cybersecurity.

The emergence of blockchain technology is the result or combination of decentralized computer networking (peer-to-peer) and communication security (cryptography). The data structure in blockchain is distributed and replicated around members in a network where each block carries a set of transactions, including hash to the previous block. The blocks that hold transactions are in the order of n, n+1, n+2, …, respectively, with backtracking due to hash included \[[11](#chapter6.xhtml#ref6_11)\]. The probability of secured transactions is possible because applications run in a decentralized fashion.

Blockchain generation offers the potential for decentralization to address the constraints of centralized digital economies and security systems. It acts as a ledger that records transactions, enabling the tracking and security of digital assets within financial networks, allowing for secure sharing in an insecure cyber environment \[[12](#chapter6.xhtml#ref6_12)\]. By eliminating the need for intermediaries such as central banks and counterparties, blockchain provides security and reliability in uncertain digital financial systems. Having a website is essential for professionals and businesses today as it provides a platform for browsing and purchasing goods and services.

The rise of online devices, online users, online shopping, online gaming, and online teaching has ultimately given rise to cyberattacks and cybercrimes \[[13](#chapter6.xhtml#ref6_13)\]. Technological advancements in cybersecurity and cyber warfare already generated many ethical issues, as internet users need increasingly ethical cyber defense strategies. The need for critical cyber defense, cybersecurity, security strategy, data privacy policy, and countermeasures is more important than ever. Current situation of cybercrime and cybersecurity efforts seems to be ineffective to solve cyber problems and disputes like online news scams, illegal e-gambling, child pornography, online tax fraud, and personal attacks on social media (see [Table 6.4](#chapter6.xhtml#tbl6_4)).

[Table 6.4](#chapter6.xhtml#b6tbl6_4) Blockchain technology features and properties for cybersecurity, cyber data, and cyber communication \[7, 10–12\]

  
| S. no. || BCT features || Feature description |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 || Trust || Customer information is now controlled by multiple trusted parties (instead of single trusted third party), hence trust is established as a primary feature |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2 || Transparency || Among participants in cyber world, blockchain can be written by anyone and viewed by anyone, providing minimum confidence and maximum transparency |
| 3 || Non-intermediaries || Blockchain technology has eliminated the need of intermediaries in several other domains, including the cyber data and cyber world |
| 4 || Privacy || Specification via smart contracts that are private and secure as one of blockchain types is a private blockchain that collaborates without disclosing data to public |
| 5 || Immutability || Customer data and customer information (also cyber data) stays forever and cannot be altered once data in blockchain is added or entered |
| 6 || Anonymity || With the help of private keys, customers/users can again establish their personal identity (anonymous), with identifiable address via the public keys |
| 7 || Cryptography || Verification of each network node as a data list (user data) is constantly added (expanded) based on the cryptography principles |
| 8 || Decentralization || Centralized storage is always risky because of central point of failure in any other cyberattacks, but here records are stored in decentralized fashion reducing risks |
| 9 || Consensus mechanism || Storing and verifying blocks of data is the primary need and the consensus mechanism enforces all participants (users) to agree on sole common fact |
| 10 || Programmability || Ability to code or program is another feature of blockchain technology; there are executable codes on how trade is to be collected among several parties |

## 6.7 Conclusion and Future Scope

Blockchain technology has a good role to play in improving cybersecurity. With its decentralized garage, immutable file, digital signatures, clever contracts, public key, cryptography, and consensus mechanisms, blockchain era affords an exceptionally comfy and transparent framework for storing and transmitting statistics. With the aid of using blockchain era, groups and individuals can ensure that their data is secure from cyberattacks and unauthorized get right of entry. The decentralized nature of the blockchain community makes it hard for hackers to get entry to and control records, and the use of digital signatures and public key cryptography ensures that facts are at ease for the duration of transmission. Using blockchain generation it is easy to relax the verbal exchange of cyber records.

There is no question that the blockchain era has already been used in lots of fields, mainly finance, supply chain, virtual marketing, IoT security, communication, and lots of greater. Due to its particular residences, the scope of the blockchain era within the zone of cybersecurity and cyber defense can be revolutionary. Regardless of its open and public nature, information can be validated and encrypted using the most comfortable cryptographic era which makes the authorization to get admission to information or facts not possible. The key power of blockchain technology is decentralization assets that noticeably will increase the abilities of the system in making security choices of its own.
