# Chapter 11Blockchain Technology Applications for E-Government and Trust

Vajratiya Vajrobol, Osamah Mohammed Jasim, Nitisha Aggarwal, Amit Pundir, Sanjeev Singh, and Geetika Jain Saxena

DOI: [10.1201/9781003449515-11](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781003449515-11)

## 11.1 Introduction: Background and Driving Forces

In the era of digital technology, E-government means electronic contact between citizens and government – an extremely basic approach. It is using information and communication technology (ICT) to make public services more effective and transparent. Through cutting the red tape, E-government not only streamlines bureaucratic procedures but also wins over member governments’ confidence. This electronically led shift of operational mode is essential for improving efficiency, transparency, and user friendliness of public services.

### 11.1.1 Definition of E-Governance

E-Governance refers to the electronic provision of support to government processes and facilitation of citizens’ participation leading to superior public services. It covers several tasks, such as online service provision, communicating digitally, and decision-making using electronic data. It should produce a responsive, answerable, and caring regime. Electronic integration includes incorporating technologies like artificial intelligence, huge information, and Internet of Things (IoT). Governments can depend on these tools to improve the ease of administration, minimise paperwork, and increase public efficiency in public services ([Al-Besher & Kumar, 2022](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_3); [Al-Mushayt, 2019](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_4)).

### 11.1.2 The Role of Trust in E-Governance

E-government thrives on trust, just like any other effective government system. Trust becomes crucial during the transformation that governments are undertaking toward digital platforms ([Srivastava & Teo, 2005](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_51)). Citizens need to know that their data is secure; they must have confidence in conducting online transactions and trust that the government is equipped to deliver services. Therefore, building trust today is focused on tackling such concerns as cybersecurity and data privacy, legal frameworks for electronic commerce, and E-government business practices. In the fast-growing electronic age, governments should strengthen regulations about government security, protection of sensitive data, and universal digitisation ([Ejdys et al., 2019](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_13)). Additionally, open communication and involved strategies are necessary. Governments can also promote inclusivity and shared responsibility by informing their citizens about the benefits of the initiatives as well as the associated risks, which completes the trust foundation.

### 11.1.3 Background and Role of Blockchain in Governing Transformation

Before the arrival of blockchain in E-government, many electronic government (E-government) programs were undertaken making use of ICT to provide public services. These moves were intended to strengthen the efficiency of government, improve service delivery, and increase transparency. However, several challenges and gaps exist in traditional E-government systems:

*   **Centralisation and Data Silos:** In fact, quite a few of these early E-government systems were centralised in nature and ended up developing data silos. Government departments often worked in silos, without interoperability and sharing of data ([Ruhode, 2013](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_42)).
    
*   **Security Concerns:** Security risks abounded from centralised databases and systems. A breach at one end of the system could leak sensitive citizen data to identity thieves and Internet criminals ([Mishra et al., 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_29)).
    
*   **Limited Trust:** Since some information is very sensitive, citizens may not want to trust centralised systems. Data privacy, data misuse, and unauthorised access are issues that could destroy public trust in E-government ([Yang et al., 2019](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_59)).
    
*   **Inefficiencies:** Despite the best intentions of E-government systems, inefficiencies remained. However, bureaucratic procedures, redundant data entry, and a lack of real-time information exchange could weaken the effectiveness of public service delivery ([Mahalli, 2015](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_28)).
    
*   **Intermediaries and Delays:** However, some E-government procedures still involved intermediaries – which delayed time and added expense. One example was that document verification and authentication often required human intervention ([Kubicek et al., 2007](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_25)).
    

Now, the introduction of blockchain in E-government addresses these gaps by offering:

*   **Decentralisation:** Due to the nature of blockchain, which lacks a central authority and allows citizens to trust this infrastructure without relying on any third party. In addition, it promotes data sharing and interoperability between government departments ([Schneider, 2019](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_45)).
    
*   **Security through Cryptography:** Blockchain's use of cryptographic techniques provides data security and integrity. Data recorded on the blockchain cannot be altered, so there's no need to worry that data might get manipulated ([Dasgupta et al., 2019](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_11)).
    
*   **Transparency:** Trust is enhanced by blockchain's transparent and auditable transactions. It means that citizens can have direct access to a secure and verifiable ledger, which eliminates the concern of hidden agendas or corruption ([Negash, 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_33)).
    
*   **Smart Contracts:** Automated and enforced by predefined rules, smart contracts eliminate the need for intermediaries in many processes. This could speed up and streamline the provision of public services ([Daramola & Thebus, 2020](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_10)).
    
*   **Citizen Empowerment:** For instance, blockchain could give citizens greater control of their data. To protect privacy, citizens can define specific permissions for data access ([Hughes et al., 2018](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_20)).
    

In this way, blockchain applications in E-government increase the efficiency, transparency, and citizen-oriented nature of a digital government infrastructure. It provides a paradigm shift by redefining trust, security, and cooperation in the provision of public services. As for E-government, blockchain can offer ingenious means of identity management and safe online voting systems, transparent processes in procurement activities, etc. ([Bodkhe et al., 2020](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_9)).

[Figure 11.1](#chapter11.xhtml#fig11_1) shows the structure of this chapter's discussion on how well-suited blockchain technology is to electronic government. The chapter communicates by first defining the concept of E-government and then focusing on how important it is to build trust in digital government exchanges. This recording and organisation system established by blockchain transforms the government itself. Blockchains have a disruptive force. A subsequent section examines how blockchain technology enables secure identity management, streamlines voting procedures, and automates government functions using smart contracts. It's not just about public service delivery but monitoring and developing resources as well; it also includes health services, education, and social support programs. These are said to include supply chain and procurement applications, property and land regulatory applications, and public financial and accounting software applications. In addition, the chapter underscores blockchain's importance in keeping data accurate and secure as well as facilitating cross-border foreign trade. There's finally a part of the problems to think about, such as coping with growth and its methodology. Lastly, this chapter gives an outline of how blockchain technology is changing how the government works in various organisations.

![Figure 11.1 Various facets of E-governance and blockchain technology.](images/fig11_1.jpg)

[Figure 11.1](#chapter11.xhtml#b11fig11_1) Various facets of E-governance and blockchain technology.

## 11.2 Transparent and Tamper-Proof Record-Keeping

Blockchain technology, which uses creative mechanisms for regulating transactions, is essential to practising transparent and unchangeable record-keeping using a digital ledger.

### 11.2.1 Designated Ledger for Record-Keeping

The old recording system isn't effective enough to stop illicit activities ([Yadav & Kushwaha, 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_58)). But blockchain offers a different kind of delivery network, with not one but thousands of copies made in networks that work together to distribute records. Because blockchain is decentralised, no one individual or entity controls the entire ledger. That's safer and more reliable. That is locked into a chain of blocks with every other transaction before any exchange takes place. Immutable data, established through an audit trail, is a key provision of this rule. It outlines the criteria for maintaining permanent records, crucial in safeguarding government employees against unfair treatment ([Jolfaei et al., 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_22)).

### 11.2.2 Prevention of Data Tampering

Blockchain restricts the ability to change data as it is designed such that no one or group of people can alter it. In this technology, the fundamental components are the “blocks” and the “chain.” A “block” within the blockchain context is essentially a compilation of digital records or transactions in [Figure 11.2](#chapter11.xhtml#fig11_2). These transactions are not standalone; rather, they are grouped together and undergo verification by participants within the network. Once this verification process is successfully completed, the block is then appended to the pre-existing chain of blocks. The term “chain” denotes the sequential arrangement of these blocks. What distinguishes blockchain is the interconnection of blocks, where each block includes a reference to the preceding one. This intricate linking of blocks forms a continuous chain, ensuring the immutability, integrity, and security of the entire transaction history. The sequential order and cryptographic links between blocks create a robust foundation for trust and transparency, addressing various challenges present in traditional centralised systems ([Politou et al., 2019](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_38)).

![Figure 11.2 The concept of block and chain in the blockchain technology.](images/fig11_2.jpg)

[Figure 11.2](#chapter11.xhtml#b11fig11_2) The concept of block and chain in the blockchain technology.

One must effect changes across the entire network all at once; otherwise, this is unrealistic or cannot happen. This way, there will be almost no data manipulation, and stakeholders to be trusted will rely on the records, leading to the truth and reliability of government records.

### 11.2.3 Transparency in Government Transactions

Blockchain keeps records of everything, and most blockchain activities can be observed. Thus, it enhances transparency, and government transactions may hardly hide the blockchain-recorded cases. Each transaction is securely documented and remains unalterable, allowing authorised individuals to easily locate and verify it ([Ølnes et al., 2017](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_37)). This high level of transparency not only fosters trust but also enables individuals to observe real-time government operations, as every transaction or operation recorded on the blockchain is visible to all participants in the network. Governance transactions can also be watched by citizens and other interested parties. This ensures that those who are involved are held accountable and makes the political process more open and better informed. Moreover, the fact that blocks are visible establishes the openness, responsiveness, and security from unauthorised changes in the system of governance. Therefore, using blockchain to maintain outdated records that cannot be changed could create a digitised log that cannot be altered. These functions complement each other to make the government records more reliable. It makes the government system more responsive and reliable ([Lykidis et al., 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_27)).

## 11.3 Security Identity Management

Focusing on E-government, security identity management is becoming increasingly important, and blockchain technology seems to be a game changer in this area. With blockchain, decentralised identity systems are also a suitable alternative to decentralised records because they are safer in case of a breach. The change makes it easier for people to keep track of their personal information, which reduces the number of cases of unauthorised access ([Elisa et al., 2023](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_14); [Sung & Park, 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_52)).

With blockchain, individuals can get their digital identity systems and control them. Blockchain surpasses conventional mechanisms for the administration of personal data, whereby administrators exercise control over private information, which is achieved through encryption. This protects people's privacy by minimising the chances of large-scale information theft that may reveal one's digital identity ([Sung & Park, 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_52)).

Secure identity management: the decentralised and cryptographic nature of blockchain provides an effective solution as follows:

1.  **Decentralised Identity Verification**
    
    Blockchain's advantage lies in decentralised identity authentication. Everyone can hold on to their personal information, and no one controls it. Users don't have to rely on a central authority to prove who they are; instead, cryptographic data saved on the blockchain can do it. It makes it less likely that there will be a single point of failure or interference.
    
2.  **Immutable Records**
    
    Because blocks are immutable, the information stored on a blockchain is resistant to tampering. After data is recorded, it cannot be changed without network consensus. This feature guarantees the integrity of identity records and precludes unauthorised changes or manipulations.
    
3.  **Encryption and Privacy**
    
    More advanced cryptographic techniques are being used to encrypt sensitive identity information, stored on the blockchain. This ensures that only authorised parties in possession of the right cryptographic keys can access and decipher the data, maximising overall privacy and security.
    
4.  **Permissioned Access**
    
    Utilising a permissioned blockchain enables control over access to identity. Only government agencies and stakeholders concerned can have access to the particulars. It makes sure that sensitive personal data does not fall into the wrong hands.
    
5.  **Smart Contracts for Access Control**
    
    Smart contracts provide the possibility to automate control of access. These contracts set out and enforce conditions about who can obtain certain identity information and under what circumstances. Automated governance means that access is granted or denied according to a set of criteria, and the chance for unauthorised entry naturally falls.
    
6.  **Biometric Authentication**
    
    Blockchain combined with biometry offers an additional security step in identity management. Biometric data, such as fingerprints or facial recognition, can be securely stored in the blockchain and is virtually impervious to fraud.
    
    When incorporated into blockchain tech applications, these measures could improve identity management security in government procedures. They aim to decrease the likelihood of hacking by external attackers. Additionally, they mitigate the risk of these systems being misused by ruling parties. This technique builds trust and openness in the E-governance system. At the same time, it prioritises protecting people's sensitive information first.
    

## 11.4 Voting Systems on the Blockchain

When applied in voting systems, blockchain technology is a significant step for free and fair elections. It sheds light on a country's political process ([Baudier et al., 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_7)). Different types of issues associated with security, fraud, and contribution have all been traced back to traditional means of voting ([Gibson et al., 2016](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_16)). However, blockchain addresses weakness by implementing a mutable, impenetrable, distributed vote log. Each transaction is independent, and no one knows about the identity of the voter and class of vote, leaving the whole process secure. This ensures transparency in the election process. For instance, in a country employing blockchain-based elections, there's a distinct scenario. Each participant possesses a unique key stored on a chain. A designated entity verifies the results, ensuring transparency. This fosters trust among the public and the electoral commission, as they collectively verify codes for reliability. It gives a sense of security that no one is being tricked, and the voting process itself plays fair. If a country uses the blockchain voting method, people living there can see for themselves that their votes have been correctly recorded and counted ([Baudier et al., 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_7); [Mohammedali & Al-Sherbaz, 2019](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_30)). This is every person's right to know about the proceedings in politics from beginning to end until the results are announced. Doing so makes citizens feel that they have acted fair. More openness and accountability towards the government will enhance public confidence in it. This would be a big step in solving the election process problems. Blockchain technology can be used for voting, which has many benefits, such as being secure, open, and unchangeable. The following is a general outline of how a vote system based on blockchain might work (see [Figure 11.3](#chapter11.xhtml#fig11_3)):

![Figure 11.3 The sequence diagram of the voting system in blockchain technology.](images/fig11_3.jpg)

[Figure 11.3](#chapter11.xhtml#b11fig11_3) The sequence diagram of the voting system in blockchain technology.

1.  **Voter Registration:**
    
    *   Citizens register on the blockchain network, providing necessary identification details to create a unique cryptographic key pair.
        
    *   The public key serves as the voter's address on the blockchain.
        
2.  **Candidate Registration:**
    
    *   Candidates are also registered on the blockchain, with their details and proposed positions.
        
3.  **Smart Contracts:**
    
    *   Smart contracts are created to define the rules of the election, including eligible voters, candidate criteria, and voting deadlines.
        
    *   These contracts are self-executing and automatically enforce the predefined rules without the need for intermediaries.
        
4.  **Voting Process:**
    
    *   Voters can cast their ballots through a secure, easy-to-use interface linked to the blockchain.
        
    *   Voting takes place on the blockchain, with each vote represented by a transaction cryptographically signed using each voter's private key.
        
5.  **Transaction Validation:**
    
    *   Each transaction is validated and approved by the network of nodes (computers) in the blockchain.
        
    *   The transaction is then added to a new block in the chain after being validated.
        
6.  **Immutable Record:**
    
    *   The immutability of the blockchain means that after a vote is entered, it cannot be changed or deleted.
        
7.  **Verification and Transparency:**
    
    *   The blockchain can be examined by voters and other stakeholders in real time to verify the election results.
        
    *   To ensure transparency, every participant can view the entire transaction history.
        
8.  **Decentralisation:**
    
    *   This decentralised nature means that there are no points of failure, making it harder to cheat.
        
9.  **Privacy Protection:**
    
    *   The blockchain allows for confidentiality while preserving the anonymity of voters. Only by their cryptographic keys are voters identified.
        
10.  **Result Declaration:**
     
     *   After the voting period ends, the smart contract tallies upvotes automatically and announces election results.
         

This blockchain voting can solve not only security and transparency concerns but also reduce fraud to protect the soundness of the democratic system. But it's important to consider factors such as user convenience and whether technical limitations may become a barrier, while also making sure that the system is both welcome and trusted by users. Also, extended testing and auditing are necessary to detect any gaps in the system before it goes live.

In election and voting processes, casting a vote means that an eligible voter indicates their choice for or sends off support to a certain candidate (or option). When voters vote, they make an official choice from the available options. With a blockchain-based voting system, voters use an interface that offers strong security and user friendliness to cast their choice. The process is frequently conducted through several cryptographic operations, designed to protect the secrecy and integrity of the vote.

## There are general steps involved in casting a vote in a blockchain-based voting system:

1.  **Voter Authentication:** The voter is authenticated by their own unique cryptographic key pair so that only authorised voters can vote.
    
2.  **Selection of Choice:** Voters select their desired candidate or option through the secure interface.
    
3.  **Cryptographic Signing:** The selected vote is then encrypted by the voter's private key. This signature is a digital certificate of the validity of one's vote.
    
4.  **Transaction on the Blockchain:** A transaction on the blockchain is used to represent a voter's cast vote. The entire transaction contains the voter's choice, cryptographic signature, and other relevant information.
    
5.  **Validation by Nodes:** The underlying network of nodes (i.e., computers) in the blockchain verifies that the vote transaction is legal and follows established rules.
    
6.  **Recording in a New Block:** After being checked, the vote transaction is added to a new block in the blockchain. This makes a record of the vote that can't be changed and is open to everyone.
    
7.  **Acknowledgement to Voter:** The blockchain system sends an acknowledgement to the voter, confirming that their vote has been successfully recorded.
    

Casting a vote is an important part of democracy, and blockchain technology is being used to make this basic political activity safer, more open, and more trustworthy.

## 11.5 Fraud Prevention in Voting

The blockchain turns out to be a good way to avoid fraud in an election. Security and honesty are some of the several problems memorised by traditional ways of voting ([Moynihan, 2004](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_32)). Blockchain resolves these issues by creating a decentralised and immutable vote record. This is mainly because blockchain technology is based on cryptography, which makes it very hard for corrupt people to tamper with the voting process by corrupting any voting data. It saves the polls and makes people believe that the voting process is fair ([Widayanti et al., 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_55); [Kagona, 2020](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_23)).

### 11.5.1 Smart Contracts: Definition and Functionality

Smart contracts that are a core part of blockchain technology revolutionise the conventional way government processes are automated. These self-executing contracts were coded with rules and conditions that had earlier been established ([Ølnes & Jansen, 2017](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_35)). If these conditions can be established, then the smart contract will be able to execute those actions agreed upon without third-party services. The underlying principle of smart contracts is to establish trust and transparency through code rather than traditional methods.

These smart contracts in government can help cut the paperwork and red tape. Smart contracts could be used in automating such activities as contract enforcement, regulatory compliance, and payment processing. This not only accelerates the process but also eliminates errors and arguments. Examples include when things are bought and smart contracts will check that they were the items ordered, paying only if a certain set of conditions is met. In addition, it allows things to be done more efficiently while making for an open and stable government ([Fiorentino & Bartolucci, 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_15)).

If many government issues can be managed by smart contracts, then intermediaries can be eliminated for efficient and fair transactions. Traditional processes create opportunities for error in the long, expensive steps between them. Smart contracts execute themselves and involve neither a middleman nor trust so that both sides can communicate with each other directly. By omitting intermediaries, these types allow for faster processes. For instance, in the case of property sales and acquisitions, smart contracts can let people change ownership automatically upon meeting certain conditions. It lessens the number of middlemen. The use of blockchain in voting systems accommodates election fraud because the record is centralised and immutable. As for smart contracts, their role is to automate government and eliminate muddlers from the process of governance. This will make the government more efficient and transparent ([Schletz et al., 2020](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_44); [Azmi et al., 2023](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_6)).

## 11.6 Public Sеrvicе Delivery and Monitoring

This big step involves including blockchain technology that makes tracking and monitoring of public service performance easier. It is not new that there are issues of integrity, accountability, and transparency that are related to some traditional approaches ([Halachmi & Greiling, 2013](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_18)). That's why blockchain is useful: it makes a permanent open register that stores all the processes of the delivery of public services. It encourages transparency and allows both the public and government agencies to monitor service delivery in real time. Such an environment is responsive and open, which makes the process of delivering public health, education, and aid programs more effective ([Abodei et al., 2019](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_1)). This ensures organisational processes remain safe to ensure services are availed easily and correctly. For example, patient information can be stored securely and passed across the healthcare system, thereby reducing running costs ([Wu et al., 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_56)). The use of blockchain in education protects and proves academic credentials, making it easier for schools and companies to check them. These resources can only reach the right people if the aid programs are transparent in their use of funds ([Raimundo & Rosário, 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_39)). It minimises waste and fraud. Blockchain use in granting public services ensures that government services work on behalf of the people with minimal wastage of resources. The government, on the other hand, ensures that it keeps records for transactions to establish the kind of services that should be offered and where government resources are channelled effectively. It could be used by legislatures to track funds use and spending in welfare programs on issues like service delivery ([Shaheen et al., 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_46); [Mohite & Acharya, 2018](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_31)). This makes the public service system too responsive and flexible, trying to meet the changing needs of people and using resources properly.

Therefore, the use of blockchain technology to provide public services is an important step. Technology can also make important things more efficient. For example, it allows one to watch and monitor government operations. This means more efficient use of resources for the public service environment.

## 11.7 Supply Chain and Procurement Management

Blockchain technology makes things transparent and simple to trace, which solves issues, such as accountability, exposure, and counterfeiting. Items come from a network-based ledger, where they cannot be altered. This makes the ledger irreversible, enabling any stakeholder to identify its items and see their history ([Raja Santhi & Muthuswamy, 2022](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_40)). This builds trust among partners and makes it easy to detect problems like recalls and quality issues immediately.

It is worth mentioning that blockchain represents an important tool that will help to lower the risk of fraud when purchasing. A decentralised record cannot be changed so that all transactions on it are verifiable. This reduces the occurrence of fraud and bribery. To begin with, when transparency in procurement, such as bid submission and contract execution, has been made public, blockchain technology is used ([Shen & Pena-Mora, 2018](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_47)). It stimulates competition and makes people answerable.

Blockchain does not stop there but further ensures that only authentic goods are allowed to work. A blockchain develops an unalterable route for the procession of a product by safeguarding all the operations it underwent throughout its production process and in distribution. This makes sure that fake products do not get into the hands of those people who do not buy them, while on the other hand, it assures these companies and governments that they are purchasing credible stuff. For instance, a business can adopt this form of technology to identify sources of raw materials and finally produce genuine and reliable final products acceptable to market standards and social ethics ([Dutta et al., 2020](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_12); [Saberi et al., 2019](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_43); [Gurtu & Johny, 2019](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_17)).

Also, blockchain will bring transparency, accountability, and trustworthiness into the present procedures of supply chain and procurement. However, blockchain makes a change as it enunciates clear supply chains, monitors procurement, removes fraud, and legalises the purchase of products and services. It brings about a shift in the rules of the game that enhances integrity, organisational efficiency, and ethical practices.

## 11.8 Property and Land Registry

Blockchain technology is changing property and land records in major ways too. A decentralised, unchangeable log that would record the proprietor of the property and allow for tracing previous owners would be implemented based on blockchain. It would make the permanent record impossible to question and therefore irrevocable. This level of openness is something people can rely on as to whether property deals could be based on facts.

Besides, blockchain is also very useful for scrapping scams and taxes with real-estate deals. It is non-centralised, making a break-in less likely while tracking the ownership. Therefore, property owners are safe from any possible lawsuits and contribute immensely to the general reliability of the property market.

The process of proving ownership of goods becomes automated through blockchain technology. Blockchain helps in making the registration process effective by improving smart contracts and decentralised confirmation. These features make the process less complicated and error-prone to a larger extent. The technology ensures the smooth running of the property registration system, thus enabling them to handle real-estate transactions correctly without difficulty ([Ameyaw & de Vries, 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_5); [Soner et al., 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_50); [Bennett et al., 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_8)). However, blockchain technology makes things transparent, avoids fraud, and speeds up the registration process. These changes combined represent a large shift in the way property issues are dealt with, to become faster, safer, and more trustworthy.

## 11.9 Public Finance Management

### 11.9.1 Clear and Unambiguous Documentation of Financial Transactions

Blockchain technology transforms the mode of transaction recording in public finance management. Through a decentralised ledger that cannot be tampered with, all financial transactions must be noted to enhance transparency. In this regard, they are more open since they give a full and unalterable account of all the revenue, expenditure, and financial activities related to governance, thereby encouraging transparency. Government officials and inspirations can get a current accurate of all financial actions. This creates mutual confidence, which significantly reduces the chances of serious financial mismanagement ([Ølnes & Jansen, 2018](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_36)).

### 11.9.2 Public Access to Real-Time Government Expenditure

Blockchain technology influences public participation as it gives people easy access to information regarding the amount of money that the government spends. Through a decentralised record, everybody can control and query how the government makes use of the funds with ease and understandably. This level of openness does not just provide individuals with authority by letting them know how they spend public funds. Rather, this makes the public smarter and more active. People power enables governments to be held accountable for how they spend money, as shown by blockchain technology. It also strengthens democratic principles applied in public financial management, leading to accountability and transparency ([Xie et al., 2019](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_57)).

### 11.9.3 Mitigation of Corruption in Public Finances

Blockchain is also a highly effective weapon to protect public funds from being stolen. With the tamper-resistant, self-controlling, and devious analysis of its blockchain technology, financial activities can be completely secure. For example, this would help to avoid crimes such as theft of funds or falsification of financial records. Furthermore, accounts of the public are reliable in that transactions on the blockchain cannot be altered. The system cannot easily be corrupted. Moreover, this move could let the public see that the government's financial system is working in a reliable and trustworthy manner. Lastly, using the blockchain system for money management makes it more transparent; people can see current data about how much a country spends and stop corruption in spending public funds. These new ideas reconstruct the structure of financial governance. They achieve this by calling for greater transparency and accountability in public property stewardship under citizen participation structures (Rien & Susilowati, 2019; [Hyvärinen et al., 2017](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_21); [Aggarwal & Floridi, 2018](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_2)).

## 11.10 Data Security and Privacy

### 11.10.1 How Blockchain Encrypts and Reaches Consensus?

Blockchain technology serves as a robust defence mechanism for data protection and privacy. Blockchain employs robust encryptions and consensus mechanisms to protect data and validate their accuracy. Safety is enhanced further by the consensus-based, decentralised process. The public ledger is formed of several blocks securely linked together with sophisticated cryptography. Combined with consensus end encryption processes, they help in creating an environment that prevents unauthorised people from changing or destroying important information and makes it very difficult to have breaches ([Shi et al., 2020](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_48)).

### 11.10.2 Capacity of the User to Control Confidential Information

Blockchain is a perfect defence when it comes to data protection and privacy. Blockchain applies robust encryption and consensus mechanisms to secure data and ensure its accuracy. In a consensus-based, decentralised process, the level of safety becomes even higher. Advanced cryptographic techniques are used to securely tie every block in the chain. Encryption and consensus processes, when used together, create a very secure environment that renders unauthorised people unable to modify or destroy vital data, hence making breaches extremely difficult ([Velliangiri et al., 2020](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_54)).

### 11.10.3 Reinforcement of Data Security Rules

Additionally, blockchain technology makes it easier to conform to and strengthen rules concerning data security. The legibility of the blockchain makes sure all the information about the exchanged data is clear and can easily be checked. This function should follow the rules of data security that people should have the right to request to remove their personal data and give clear consent for any edition. Through blockchain, organisations can prove that they are following the law, thus building trust among the public sector and users ([Zhang et al., 2018](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_60)).

Through encryption and consensus, the users are provided with more control over who may access their private data; at the same time, their saved information is stored in a much safer manner. It's the same with features that make blockchain conformist, which also happens to simplify satisfying legal requirements. It helps the data be handled in a trustworthy environment and protects privacy.

## 11.11 Transboundary Exchange, Interoperability

### 11.11.1 Employing Blockchain across International Borders

Therefore, blockchain technology works as a great choice for safeness and novel methods to cross-borders. Blockchain ensures the safety and purity of cross-border transactions by keeping records distributed and immutable. Since the blockchain log is public and cannot be altered, it is the safest way to record and affirm the transactions. It also reduces the risk of fraud and ensures that cross-border financial activity is trusted. This also makes deals safer yet helps in building trust in financial transactions ([Sinha & Roy Chowdhury, 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_49)).

### 11.11.2 Better Ability of Government Agencies to Work Together

Blockchain technology makes it possible for these two governments to work together, thus creating channels and removing the silos in their systems. Since blockchain is a decentralised structure, different government departments can exchange data openly and safely. The compatibility enables essential information to be shared quickly, hence solving routine problems and improving foreign governments. In this way, customs and immigration offices can safely share information, which paves the way for foreign trade and travel ([Okazaki, 2018](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_34)).

### 11.11.3 Improving Trade and Cooperation All over the World

Blockchain technology could also enhance the way foreign trade and cooperation are conducted. The features of blockchain in smart contracts and open validation make complicated transnational processes easier to handle and manage. Also, this technology speeds up the business process with other countries, reducing the likelihood of errors and disagreements. This technology makes foreign businesses more efficient and accountable in a clear record of every step of the procedure, which can be checked up. It promotes cooperation and makes the economy stronger ([Tian et al., 2022](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_53)). Using blockchain technology for connectivity and cross-border transactions makes financial transactions safer, allows government agencies to talk to each other more easily, and speeds up the process of doing business and collaborating across borders. These changes make the world a better place to work together, open, and become efficient.

## 11.12 Challenges and Considerations

### 11.12.1 Demand Management; Debottlenecking and Efficiency

In terms of scale and speed, blockchain technology has many obstacles to overcome; the key issue is scaling. This scalability issue is the most important since it determines how many transactions a given blockchain can handle. If the network is congested, then blockchain will be slower with lower transaction throughputs at higher costs. Contemporary strategies, including sharding and layer-two scaling solutions, must be used to resolve scalability problems ([Khan et al., 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_24)).

### 11.12.2 Standardisation for Ensuring Wide-Scale Acceptance

The government would have to define clear regulations for blockchain implementation on a large scale. Many different consensus methods can be used for blockchain systems, as well as the various computational languages of smart contracts and interoperability standards ([Llamas Covarrubias & Llamas Covarrubias, 2021](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_26)). The absence of clear standards would also make for joint work and difficulties in connecting different blockchain networks. It is necessary to establish common requirements for different systems to be interoperable, thereby promoting environmental stability conducive to the broadest acceptance of these systems.

### 11.12.3 Strategies to Improve Public Awareness and Education

If the regulations concerning blockchain technology are introduced, then it can be used for large-scale purposes. Consensus protocols may be used with blockchain systems, which have various interoperability standards and smart contract computational languages. However, without such clear standards, people will find it hard to work together due to the lack of a way to connect multiple blockchains in one easy step. Therefore, it requires the setting of standards that everybody abides by so that there is interoperability and stability required for widespread acceptance ([Holotescu, 2018](#chapter11.xhtml#ref11_19)).

## 11.13 Conclusion

In the area of E-governance, blockchain technology has brought about a dramatic change in citizen engagement with the government. Applications of this technology now range from secured international transactions through enhanced data security and privacy to the creation of virtually indestructible records. For instance, some of the functions of blockchain technology include smart contracts for automatic tasks, prevention of vote fraud, and ensuring public services are provided and monitored well. Blockchain technology also ensures precision in land and property registration, streamlines management of public funds, and safeguards the security of voter systems.

There is an enormous power that blockchain offers for the future of governments. With time, blockchain is expected to greatly influence how governments work by making them more open and establishing public trust. Currently, blockchain technology is being used to build E-governance that can make the government more transparent, efficient, and focused on citizens's demands. The versatility and freedom of this technology indicate a future wherein the blockchain is a vital aspect of running the government digitally.

With the wide adoption of blockchain technology, as countries get used to the digital world, the government should understand that blockchain technology is key to building confidence, discouraging fraud, and improving processes generally. Using this technology for voting purposes or public finance can make the government more flexible and stable. Lawmakers, scientists, and ordinary people should strive together for the standardisation of the problems to solve them. They should work together towards a future where E-governance and blockchain make the world safer and embrace technology.

The future of E-governance holds significant promise for blockchain technology, even though it is relatively new. By exploring innovative ideas, coordinating activities, and committing to the use of advanced technologies, governments have the potential to make the relationship between people and governing bodies more transparent, efficient, and trustworthy.

# Chapter 12Blockchain-Based Secure Digital Library System

Shallu Sharma and Balraj Singh

DOI: [10.1201/9781003449515-12](https://doi.org/10.1201/9781003449515-12)

## 12.1 Introduction

The service work in the library field itself is extremely comprehensive, and the scope of work involved is also extremely wide. In addition, in modern society, people's desire for various cultural knowledge is even stronger. The library can also provide a large number of high-quality books, which causes the library to introduce more high-quality books, and the number of readers who need services every day also shows a significant increase. This invisibly increases the difficulty of library services. To make the original service work with a higher quality, relevant staff can actively apply blockchain technology to optimize the past work links so that the work of the entire library field is more orderly. This chapter analyzes and summarizes the working methods of the library at this stage and proposes some practical work plans, aimed at helping more staff to actively apply blockchain technology to reshape the working environment when performing work tasks \[[1](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_1)\]. This requires staff to actively use their spare time to research and learn more information technology and create a better service system based on the actual situation of the library and the real demands of readers \[[2](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_2)\]. This can make the operation of the library novel so that people from all walks of life can improve their comprehensive literacy by reading books. The staff in the library field understand that the quality of their work can directly affect the quality of teaching and employment. Meanwhile, the maintenance of mobile readers is extremely important for the work in the library field \[[3](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_3)\]. The emergence of mobile readers and the increase in the types of readers will also promote data information with more obvious characteristics of complexity. Therefore, to be able to provide readers with better services, the library must use this data information reasonably. These data resources can also become an important basis for libraries to build online service platforms and improve service links, allowing big data technology to play its important role. In this way, blockchain technology can also show great advantages in good corporations.

## 12.2 Motivation

Security of data is a prime issue in document delivery which further has given rise to problems such as resource theft and unauthorized attacks. Most traditional document delivery methods are offline to ensure data security \[[4](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_4), [5](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_5)\]. These methods are not very real-time and can easily cause data loss. With the constant development of technology, online transmission of data becomes advantageous in terms of timeliness. However, current interlibrary document access and authorization systems are designed for centralized servers and distributed databases \[[5](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_5)\]. This may cause unauthorized access to data and privacy breaches. In addition to this, central servers are prone to hacking, bringing the problem of single-point failure, which results in the non-availability of resources. This jeopardizes the security of data and calls for third-party dependencies. Though many researchers are working on various factors of this problem, one of the biggest reasons for its failure is that organizations are reluctant to provide access to non-members \[[6](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_6), [7](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_7)\]. Therefore, a well-designed schema is needed to handle the authorization and provide a solution of access control with decentralization. The introduction of blockchain will be a breakthrough with an effective contribution to the services of a smart resource system. A traditional library may suffer from physical damages of property vandalism, while since the branches are distanced physically, it can be considered as a limited accident. Being interconnected and reachable with clicks, a digital library's information infrastructure can suffer from a DDoS attack, which may bring down the entire service \[[8](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_8)\]. A decentralized network displays its advantage by separating the risk from the central to multiple small branches. Hence, bringing down a few service nodes does not affect the service provided by the entire network. Providing a system with the ease to expand and ubiquitous characteristics without a particular centralized service point is the main design philosophy. We are mostly concerned with the criticality of service availability and efficiency required by a library, but access control and right management are other functionalities that are as critical as service availability \[[9](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_9)\]. The traditional library, though, may suffer from unauthorized access to resources. The burdens of physical copy can make it difficult for the threat to influence further. As a comparison, for a digitalized library, unauthorized access is more critical: mismanagement and altered records can tremendously tamper with the assets’ legality \[[10](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_10), [11](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_11)\].

We summarize the needs for a next-generation library as follows.

1.  **Large concurrent access session**: The system should handle intense concurrent access sessions to fulfill the customers’ requests and be immune from denial-of-service attacks \[[12](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_12)\].
    
2.  **Storage efficiency:** The system is able to store and distribute data efficiently, in which the file system is designed to lower the possible chances of data duplication. At the same time, the system can provide fast data retrieval \[[13](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_13)\].
    
3.  **Easy to expand:** The operator can quickly expand the storage capacity with few setup steps without interrupting the service \[[14](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_14)\].
    
4.  **Security:** Tampering access control should be challenging. The system is able to provide a robust user access control of each archive \[[15](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_15)\].
    

## 12.3 Blockchain

Cryptocurrencies are one of the vital examples that noted the importance of providing a secure and trusted data store in a decentralized system. It requires every participant to keep a ledger on their local premises. Anyone who breaks the consensus and alters some ledger copies can cause severe damage to the platform's credit \[[16](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_16), [17](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_17)\]. Ensuring a unified and approved version of the copy in multiple decentralized points is crucial (see [Figure 12.1](#chapter12.xhtml#fig12_1)).

![Figure 12.1 Blockchain functional structure.](images/fig12_1.jpg)

[Figure 12.1](#chapter12.xhtml#b12fig12_1) Blockchain functional structure.

## 12.4 Issues and Challenges to Blockchain in Library

Blockchain technology is still in its experimental beginnings and must confront a lot of problems, even though there is a great probability that it would disrupt the education system \[[18](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_18)\]. Some of them are caused by the immaturity of technology, while others are caused by circumstances that are not related to technology. [Table 12.1](#chapter12.xhtml#tbl12_1) analysis how blockchain may solve some conventional issues during its implementation.

[Table 12.1](#chapter12.xhtml#b12tbl12_1) Blockchain challenges in library services

  
| S.N. || Conventional issues || Blockchain solution |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 1 || Huge storage required \[[17](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_17)\] || Data is recorded in blocks; decentralized storage as IPFS |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 2 || Data addressing safety is low \[[19](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_19)\] || Cryptography algorithms are supported by blockchain |
| 3 || No policy protection or guidance \[[20](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_20)\] || Smart contracts to implement policies and attributes |
| 4 || Privacy of the user || Cryptography with time stamping |
| 5 || Confidentiality of data || Consensus algorithms |
| 6 || Availability of resources \[[21](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_21)\] || Certificate authority |
| 7 || Security of contents \[[22](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_22)\] || Permissioned and permission-less access |

## 12.5 Types of Blockchain Network

Blockchain is a decentralized arrangement that permits to creation a digital distributed record of transactions and sharing it among various nodes on the network in place of storing it at a central server. A decentralized network divides the burden to each node and uses their processing capability to do so. In online resource sharing, decentralization systems are more scalable than centralized ones \[[23](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_23)\]. Blockchain networks are basically categorized in three sections as shown in [Figure 12.2](#chapter12.xhtml#fig12_2).

![Figure 12.2 Types of blockchain network.](images/fig12_2.jpg)

[Figure 12.2](#chapter12.xhtml#b12fig12_2) Types of blockchain network.

## 12.6 Proposed Approach

Reviews of the literature indicate that researchers have been given their best to build an effective distributed database system to support data sharing within multiple platforms from the very beginning. The concept of blockchain technology is in its early era in library and information science. Blockchain technology chains a decentralized network where there is no sole owner of the network. A block in the network can enclose the chain of transactions. Data privacy is ensured by encrypting blocks data. The user owns the private key only whereas public key is associated with all the participating nodes of the network \[[23](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_23), [24](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_24)\]. The transaction created by the user is encrypted with their private key. Consequently, the resultant process creates a public key that is linked with the transaction, and the process is irreversible to secure the private key of the user. On the other hand, the nodes in the network use the public key to authorize transactions in the network on the basis of consensus protocol \[[25](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_25)\]. Moreover, with each new transaction initiated in the blockchain, it is added to the previously stored ledgers on all the member nodes in the network. The proposal is made based on the blockchain network for the electronic library record-sharing strategy. The blockchain environment makes it easier to share resources with inapproachable patrons in an economical way.

## 12.7 Design of Smart Contract

In our proposed architecture, smart contract-based Ethereum network is used to portrait the existing interlibrary service records upon each participating node on the network. The blockchain transactions are accompanied by cryptographically encrypted signature for managing the ownership of data, permissions to access that data and integrity of the data \[[24](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_24)\].

Only the legitimated transactions are carried out by the state-transactions functions of smart contract for executing data alteration. These rules are formed to regulate the workflow of book sharing system. This framework is designed to implement smart contract for complex data transfers and maintaining the security and privacy of data sources \[[26](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_26)\]. The smart contract is designed to handle all the states from conferring permission to data access as shown in [Figure 12.3](#chapter12.xhtml#fig12_3).

![Figure 12.3 Smart contract workflow of interlibrary service.](images/fig12_3.jpg)

[Figure 12.3](#chapter12.xhtml#b12fig12_3) Smart contract workflow of interlibrary service.

## 12.8 User Validation and System Authentication Algorithms

In the proposed work, user validation within the library ecosystem is taken to the next level through a combination of generated authentication keys and user location data. These authentication keys, unique to each user and based on their placement in the library environment, serve as the initial layer of security and access control \[[27](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_27)\]. However, to further enhance validation, the system leverages polynomial curve fitting. This mathematical technique is employed to establish a robust relationship between user locations and their authentication keys. Polynomial curve fitting generates mathematical models that accurately represent how each user's location within the library ecosystem corresponds to their authentication key. During validation, when a user attempts to access library resources or perform actions, their precise location data is collected and fed into the previously established polynomial equation. If the evaluated equation closely matches their authentication key or falls within an acceptable tolerance, the user is considered valid, granting them access to the requested resources. This approach not only verifies the authentication key but also validates the user's proximity pattern, significantly enhancing the security and access control mechanism.

The incorporation of polynomial curve fitting for validation adds an extra layer of security and precision to the library's access control system. It ensures that users are authenticated not solely based on their keys but also by considering their physical proximity to library resources. Even minor deviations in a user's location data will result in distinct polynomial equations, rendering unauthorized access attempts challenging. This innovative approach contributes to a more robust and reliable authentication system within the library ecosystem, offering enhanced security and ensuring that only legitimate users with matching proximity patterns are granted access to the library's valuable resources.

1.  **Initialization**: The algorithm starts by setting certain parameters and initializing variables. It specifies the polynomial order (**ord**), which is used for polynomial curve fitting. It also extracts cluster information (**clus**) from some **place** variable and sets the number of generations (**gen**) to 10.
    
2.  **Main Loop (Generations)**: The algorithm enters a loop that runs for a specified number of generations (**gen**). This loop appears to be part of an iterative process, possibly for data validation or trust establishment.
    
3.  **Trust Calculation**: Within each generation, the algorithm calculates a set of trusted members (**trust**) based on some criteria involving student and cloud information (**s****t****u**[**x**], **s****t****u**[**y**], **c****l****u****s**, **c****l****o****u****d**[**x**], **c****l****o****u****d**[**y**]). The specifics of how trust is calculated are not detailed in the provided code.
    
    ![](images/ufig12_1.jpg)
4.  **Trust Table**: The trusted members are stored in a trust table (**t****r****u****s****t**[**t****a****b**]), which includes information about their coordinates and keys. This trust table seems to be used for subsequent operations.
    
5.  **Active Student Selection**: A portion of active students is selected based on some criteria. These active students are identified by their indices (**a****c****t**[**l****i****s****t**]), and the selection process appears to involve randomness.
    
6.  **Library Cluster Assignment**: The algorithm assigns each active student to a library cluster (**a****c****t**[**l****i****b**]). This assignment seems to be based on the **clus** information.
    
7.  **Unique Libraries**: The unique libraries (**libs**) within the clusters are identified. This suggests that the algorithm is working with multiple libraries or nodes within the network.
    
8.  **Inner Loop (Libraries)**: The algorithm enters another loop that iterates through the identified unique libraries (**libs**).
    
9.  **Library Key**: For each library, the library's key is retrieved (**l****i****b**[**k**]).
    
10.  **Request Handling**: There is a section of code that seems to handle requests from active students. It checks if the number of requests is less than a specified polynomial order (**ord**). If not, it adds requests until the condition is met. This part appears to simulate request handling or data validation within the network.
     
11.  **Data Validation**: If requests are raised, data validation is performed. It involves gathering data from active students (**d****a****t**[**v****a****l**]) and comparing it with validated data (**v****a****l**[**d****a****t**]) from the trust table. Polynomial curve fitting (**f****i****t**[**c****u****r****v**]) is then applied to these data sets. The specific purpose and interpretation of this curve fitting are not detailed in the provided code.
     
12.  **End of Inner Loop**: The inner loop concludes for the current library, and the algorithm proceeds to the next library or cluster.
     
13.  **End of Generation**: After all libraries have been processed within a generation, the algorithm continues to the next generation until the specified number of generations (**gen**) is reached.
     

Once the user is authenticated, the user requests for resources from its respective library (see [Figure 12.4](#chapter12.xhtml#fig12_4)).

![Figure 12.4 Generated keys.](images/fig12_4.jpg)

[Figure 12.4](#chapter12.xhtml#b12fig12_4) Generated keys.

## 12.9 Implementations and Results

A digital library is a collection of learning resources in electronic format. The user can access them online at his convenience. In such cases, data security and availability are important issues faced by these libraries, especially when the resources are expensive and sometimes, scarce. We established Ethereum smart contracts to avail of interlibrary loan service and document delivery. Smart contracts permit the user to run the code on a blockchain network with a set of rules in agreement by all participating parties. This will eliminate the role of the third party in document delivery and libraries can share their resources directly on the network with the users \[[28](#chapter12.xhtml#ref12_28)\].

Our proposed framework includes of main components as participant/entities, smart contracts, and blockchain network. A smart contract is a combination of functions and their states residing at a particular address on the Ethereum blockchain. The code in [Figure 12.5](#chapter12.xhtml#fig12_5) shows instantiates and deploys a contract to execute an account on Ethereum.

![Figure 12.5 Smart contract to execute an account on network.](images/fig12_5.jpg)

[Figure 12.5](#chapter12.xhtml#b12fig12_5) Smart contract to execute an account on network.

The participating entities in the proposed model are admin, user, and library. Every entity has a diverse role and can perform variant tasks by entreating the smart contract functions. In the given model, the user login to the portal using a username and password. If user credentials are valid, he enters the Ethereum network. This generates the genesis block in the chain. The user can search for the required file using the “search option”. If the file exists, the contract address is assigned to the file, and the hash code will be generated. This hash code is shared with the user first. The whole transaction is stored in the ledger as a block. In case the user is not authorized to access the file, he will be requested to register with the network. This way, multiple users can request the shared books and access them after verification.

## 12.10 User Login and Registration

In our model, the user login to the portal using a username and password. If user credentials are valid, he enters the Ethereum network. Through the user interface, a user authenticates his credentials. The data access is granted only after successful authentication. The interface forwards the request and server logs grant or revokes the permission in the blockchain. The server updates the response and notifies the user. This generates the genesis block in the chain. [Figure 12.6](#chapter12.xhtml#fig12_6) shows the user interface to register/log in to the network.

![Figure 12.6 User registration and login.](images/fig12_6.jpg)

[Figure 12.6](#chapter12.xhtml#b12fig12_6) User registration and login.

As seen in [Figure 12.6](#chapter12.xhtml#fig12_6), when the user tries to login or register him to the network, a genesis block is created by smart contract and its hash code is generated. [Figure 12.7](#chapter12.xhtml#fig12_7) shows the genesis block with its address.

![Figure 12.7 Genesis block to initiate the network.](images/fig12_7.jpg)

[Figure 12.7](#chapter12.xhtml#b12fig12_7) Genesis block to initiate the network.

The user can search for the required file using the “search option”. If the file exists, the contract address is assigned to the file, and the hash code will be generated. This hash code is shared with the user first. The whole transaction is stored in the ledger as a block. The geographically distant libraries are located on the network in [Figure 12.8](#chapter12.xhtml#fig12_8). The registered user searches for a book and its location is detected by the contract.

![Figure 12.8 Smart contract execution for file search.](images/fig12_8.jpg)

[Figure 12.8](#chapter12.xhtml#b12fig12_8) Smart contract execution for file search.

During this, a contract address is assigned to the transaction and transaction is recorded in a block with its hash code as shown in [Figure 12.7](#chapter12.xhtml#fig12_7). The data is encrypted and only hash code address is shared to the user. This keep the data secured from any attack. In case the user is not authorized to access the file, he will be requested to register with the network. This way, multiple users can request the shared books and access after verification.

Instead of original data, only its hash code is shared with the user. This keeps the data safe from unauthorized attacks and transaction is recorded in blocks on the chain. The overall mining can be seen in [Figure 12.9](#chapter12.xhtml#fig12_9), where only the transaction address is referred to in place of the data. Thus, only users demanding the resources will get access to it and the privacy and security of all the entities are maintained.

![Figure 12.9 Transaction details on Ethereum network.](images/fig12_9.jpg)

[Figure 12.9](#chapter12.xhtml#b12fig12_9) Transaction details on Ethereum network.

## 12.11 Simulation Settings

Initial smart contract testing is done with Etherscan. The transaction time calculates the performance of the given network. [Figures 12.10](#chapter12.xhtml#fig12_10) and [12.11](#chapter12.xhtml#fig12_11) show the number of transactions committed and transaction throughput with 1org1peer and 2org1peer combinations, respectively. The simulation system configurations have items as shown in [Table 12.2](#chapter12.xhtml#tbl12_2).

[Figure 12.12](#chapter12.xhtml#b12fig12_12) Gas price vs transaction amount.

## 12.12 Conclusion and Future Work

Through this chapter, the current problems faced by libraries while sharing resources are discussed. An Ethereum-based framework is explained with participating entities (user, library, and eBooks) in an immutable and transparent environment for trusted and secure sharing of learning resources. An access control mechanism to maintain the privacy and security of all entities is explained with a set of algorithms while sharing the data between the participants. This helps to reduce the dependencies on central controls and the risk of single-point breakdown. The system provides security through a tamperproof immutable ledger as the unauthorized user cannot alter the ledger. Additionally, blockchain technology provides a decentralized environment to the system where multiple users can access the data from different locations and protection in a trusted atmosphere without any fear of information loss. The evaluation and experiment of the proposed system will be achieved in upcoming work with overall parameter metrics such as throughput, average delay, latency, and CPU usage of the proposed work.
